\/ Spinsopotamia and the NALMI Casino Network \u2013 New Compliance Intelligence Report Released"},"content":{"rendered":"\nPublic\u2011source OSINT model links a Zentoria\u2011facing billing descriptor into a tightly concentrated casino infrastructure \u2013 without yet proving ownership or a single UBO.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFor months, FinTelegram has been tracking how Irish\u2011licensed bookmaker Zentoria Limited<\/strong> and its billing descriptor \u201cSpinsopotamia.com Dublin\u201d<\/strong> appear in connection with offshore casino brands within the NovaForge<\/strong> network. Earlier reporting showed how deposits into brands such as Robycasino<\/strong> and Spinsy<\/strong> surfaced Zentoria and Spinsopotamia on player card statements, positioning the combo as an EU\u2011facing payment fa\u00e7ade rather than a conventional operator brand.<\/p>\n\n\n\nA newly completed FinTelegram Compliance Intelligence Report<\/strong> now adds a dense technical OSINT layer to this story. Based on four isolated collection runs and 496 casino\u2011related domains, the report maps how the Zentoria\u2011facing anchor SPINSOPOTAMIA.COM<\/strong> currently resolves into the same NALMI \/ AS213846 \u2013 185.207.196.0\/22<\/strong> infrastructure envelope as 495 of 496<\/strong> investigated domains, with 120 byte\u2011identical application\u2011asset groups<\/strong> connecting 98 of them into 16 deployment components across 29 brand families.<\/p>\n\n\n\nKey findings \u2013 Zentoria \/ Spinsopotamia \/ NALMI report<\/h2>\n\n\n\n\nSPINSOPOTAMIA.COM<\/strong>, used as billing descriptor and casino entry point for Zentoria Limited, currently resolves to 185.207.197.216, inside the same 185.207.196.0\/22, AS213846 (NALMI) infrastructure envelope as 495 of 496 investigated casino\u2011related domains.<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe dominant casino endpoints<\/strong> sit in 185.207.196.0\/24 (491 domains), with only a handful of domains in 197\/24, 198\/24 and 199\/24 \u2013 indicating a highly concentrated casino infrastructure routed through a single provider and prefix.<\/li>\n\n\n\nAfter removing generic Cloudflare and third\u2011party assets, 120 meaningful SHA\u2011256 hash groups were identified<\/strong>, forming 16 technical components that connect 98 domains across 29 observed brand families via byte\u2011identical first\u2011party application bundles.<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe largest application component links 36 domains from ten brands<\/strong> (including BoaBoa, Boomerang Casino, BuranCasino, Cadoola, CasinoInfinity, CasinoUnlimited, Castyr, Fezbet, Librabet, Rabona) through dozens of identical JavaScript bundles, consistent with a shared multi\u2011skin casino platform.<\/li>\n\n\n\nFifty\u2011one domains across fourteen brand families expose identical Sentry Replay sampling values<\/strong> (for both session and error replay), providing a telemetry\u2011layer correlation that overlaps with the application\u2011layer components and reinforces the shared\u2011platform pattern.<\/li>\n\n\n\nPlatform and service\u2011layer artefacts (including an explicit SPTPub host and recurring integrations such as analytics, Hotjar\u2011style behavioural tools, SEON, MiFinity, PaymentIQ, Anjouan components, Sportradar, Zendesk and mail providers) point to a templated, centralised casino\u2011platform environment used across many brands.<\/li>\n\n\n\nSpinsopotamia.com itself did not produce meaningful shared\u2011asset hashes in the analysed snapshot due to incomplete public asset collection; its correlation with the wider network is therefore established through DNS, prefix and ASN rather than application\u2011layer matches, and this absence is treated as incomplete data, not as disproof.<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe report uses a tiered evidence weighting system<\/strong> (PRIMARY\/SECONDARY\/LEAD\/BACKGROUND) and explicitly warns against silently converting a match on one layer (e.g. prefix, application bundle, telemetry configuration) into presumed matches on every other layer.<\/li>\n\n\n\nLegally, the report\u00a0does not<\/strong>\u00a0claim common ownership of all 496 domains, does not identify a single UBO, and does not assert that Zentoria Limited controls all NALMI customer accounts or that any criminal or regulatory breach has been proven; it instead defines a high\u2011correlation infrastructure environment and a set of high\u2011value disclosure targets.<\/li>\n\n\n\nPrimary disclosure targets identified include NALMI (185.207.196.0\/22 customer and abuse records), Cloudflare (zone and origin history), SPTPub and other platform\/telemetry\/support providers (tenant ownership and authorised users), and payment\/cashier providers (MIDs, routing parameters, acquirers, and settlement beneficiaries), which together hold the records needed to move from technical correlation to any legal attribution.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nRead our Zentoria Reports here. <\/a><\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nHigh correlation \u2013 without overstepping into ownership claims<\/h2>\n\n\n\nAt the infrastructure layer, the strongest finding is concentration, not exact\u2011IP identity<\/strong>. The majority of observed casino endpoints sit in 185.207.196.0\/24<\/strong>, while Spinsopotamia.com currently resolves to 185.207.197.216<\/strong> \u2013 a different host, but within the same 185.207.196.0\/22<\/strong> prefix and the same NALMI provider envelope. This creates a narrow, clearly defined target area for regulators and payment institutions: an entire casino ecosystem routed through one infrastructure provider, with the Zentoria\u2011facing descriptor sitting inside that same block.<\/p>\n\n\n\nAbove that, the new report documents 120 meaningful cross\u2011domain SHA\u2011256 hash groups<\/strong> that bind 98 domains into 16 application\u2011layer components, including large clusters spanning brands such as BoaBoa, Boomerang Casino, BuranCasino, Cadoola, CasinoInfinity, Casinoly, Joker8, Frumzi, BetRiot, GreatWin, PowerUpCasino, ExciteWin, Playzilla, PolestarCasino, FunID, LegendPlay, PowBet<\/strong> and others. Fifty\u2011one domains across fourteen brand families also expose identical Sentry Replay sampling values<\/strong>, while recurring platform and service\u2011layer components (for example SPTPub<\/strong>, MiFinity, SEON, analytics and support tooling) point to a templated, centralised casino\u2011platform environment<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nCrucially, the report is explicit about what it does not<\/strong> do. It does not establish<\/strong> that Zentoria Limited legally owns or operates every domain in the NALMI \/22 cluster; it does not identify a single ultimate beneficial owner; and it does not assert criminal or regulatory liability as a matter of law. Instead, it converts a very large body of public\u2011source technical evidence into a compact correlation model and a set of primary disclosure targets<\/strong> \u2013 notably NALMI as infrastructure provider, Cloudflare at the edge, SPTPub as platform host, Sentry and Zendesk as telemetry\/support tenants, and the payment and wallet providers underpinning the cashier layer.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhy this matters for regulators and payment institutions<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFor gambling and financial regulators, acquiring banks, wallet providers, and card schemes, the significance is twofold. First, the infrastructure and application\u2011layer correlation narrows down where to ask questions: who controls the customer accounts behind 185.207.196.0\/22, who owns the platform tenants, and who benefits from the settlement flows behind these rotating casino domains. Second, the presence of a licensed EU bookmaker at the visible payment layer \u2013 Zentoria Limited<\/strong> \u2013 raises classic questions about shadow processing<\/strong>, descriptor\u2011laundering and mixed\u2011risk merchant portfolios, similar to patterns previously observed in other FinTelegram cases.<\/a><\/p>\n\n\n\nThe new report is therefore not a verdict but a map<\/strong>: it highlights the rails, entities, and ecosystems that regulators and PSP compliance teams should now interrogate with their own legal powers and customer\u2011information records.<\/a><\/p>\n\n\n\nDownload the full report<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has decided to make the full \u201cZentoria \/ Spinsopotamia and the NALMI Casino Network\u201d Compliance Intelligence Report<\/strong> available as a downloadable document for regulators, payment institutions, banks, crypto exchanges, legal counsel, and investigative media partners.<\/a><\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\nDownload the Zentoria \/ NALMI Compliance Report<\/a><\/div>\n<\/div>\n<\/div>\n\n\n\n\n\nDownload the Technical Annex to the Compliance Report<\/a><\/div>\n<\/div>\n<\/div>\n<\/div>\n\n\n\nThe report sets out the methodology, data scope, infrastructure and application\u2011layer findings, telemetry and platform\u2011layer markers, a detailed correlation matrix, and a dedicated limitations and legal\u2011boundary section.<\/a><\/p>\n\n\n\nCall for whistleblowers: help close the gaps<\/h2>\n\n\n\nAs always, public\u2011source OSINT can only go so far. Secures Austrian MiCA License \u2014 Vienna\u2019s FMA Emerges As A Key Gateway For Global Crypto Exchanges"},"content":{"rendered":"\nBy FinTelegram Editorial Desk<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nThe Austrian Financial Market Authority (FMA)<\/strong> has granted a MiCAR authorisation<\/a> to WB-Shield Innovations GmbH<\/a><\/strong>, the Austrian entity operating as WhiteBIT EU<\/a><\/strong>. The approval strengthens WhiteBIT<\/strong>\u2019s regulated European footprint and confirms Vienna\u2019s growing role as a MiCA gateway for major crypto platforms. However, the authorised service scope deserves close reading. FMA has alreay issued several MiCA licences over the last couple of months to large crypto exchange operators, including KuCoin<\/strong> or ByBit<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nKey Findings<\/h2>\n\n\n\n\nWhiteBIT EU has received a MiCAR authorisation in Austria<\/strong> through WB-Shield Innovations GmbH<\/strong>, registered under FN 649506g<\/strong>.<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe FMA authorisation covers custody<\/strong>, crypto\/fiat exchange<\/strong>, crypto\/crypto exchange<\/strong>, placement of crypto-assets<\/strong>, and transfer services<\/strong>.<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe published FMA scope does not explicitly list \u201coperation of a trading platform\u201d<\/strong> or \u201cexecution of orders\u201d as authorised services.<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhiteBIT positions itself as one of Europe\u2019s largest crypto exchanges, part of W Group<\/strong>, with a major global user base and strong sports-sponsorship visibility.<\/li>\n\n\n\nAustria\u2019s FMA has become a significant MiCA licensing venue, with previous authorisations including Bitpanda<\/strong>, Bybit EU<\/strong>, KuCoin EU<\/strong>, AMINA Austria<\/strong>, Coinfinity<\/strong>, FIOR Digital<\/strong>, DADAT Krypto<\/strong>, Cryptonow<\/strong>, and Validvent<\/strong>.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nThe WhiteBIT Licence<\/h2>\n\n\n\n<\/figure>\n<\/div>\n\n\nThe Austrian FMA has granted WB-Shield Innovations GmbH<\/strong> authorisation as a crypto-asset service provider under Article 63 MiCAR. The entity operates as WhiteBIT EU<\/strong> and is positioned as the regulated European vehicle for the WhiteBIT group.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhiteBIT has announced the preparation of whitebit.eu<\/strong>, a dedicated platform for users in the European Economic Area. This is a strategically important step: once properly passported and operationalised, a MiCAR authorisation from one EU member state can become the regulatory basis for broader EEA market access.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFrom a compliance perspective, the exact service perimeter matters. The FMA\u2019s published authorisation covers custody and administration, exchange against funds, exchange against other crypto-assets, placement, and transfer services. It does not, based on the published notice, expressly include the operation of a crypto-asset trading platform. FinTelegram will therefore monitor whether the final WhiteBIT EU offering is structured as a brokerage\/exchange service within the authorised scope or whether additional permissions become relevant.<\/p>\n\n\n\nMarket Position<\/h2>\n\n\n\nWhitebit founder Volodymyr Vosov<\/figcaption><\/figure>\n<\/div>\n\n\nWhiteBIT was founded in 2018 and is led by the Ukrainian Volodymyr Nosov<\/a><\/strong>, founder and president of W Group<\/a><\/strong>. The company presents itself<\/a> as the largest European crypto exchange by traffic and part of a broader fintech ecosystem serving more than 35 million customers globally.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe group has built a strong public brand through sports and entertainment partnerships, including FC Barcelona<\/strong>, Juventus<\/strong>, FACEIT<\/strong>, and the Ukrainian national football ecosystem. These partnerships have given WhiteBIT visibility far beyond the crypto-native trading audience.<\/p>\n\n\n\nPublic market-position claims should nevertheless be treated with caution. Crypto-exchange rankings are volatile and depend heavily on methodology, reported volume, traffic metrics, liquidity scoring, and wash-trading controls. For compliance analysis, the more relevant question is not only size, but whether governance, AML, sanctions controls, market-integrity monitoring, custody safeguards, complaints handling, and cross-border onboarding are robust enough for EEA operations under MiCAR.<\/p>\n\n\n\nKey People And Austrian Entity<\/h2>\n\n\n\nPerson \/ Entity<\/th>Role<\/th>Relevance<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>Volodymyr Nosov<\/a><\/strong>(website<\/a>)<\/td>Founder \/ President of W Group; founder\/CEO figure behind WhiteBIT<\/td>Strategic leader and public face of the WhiteBIT ecosystem<\/td><\/tr>WB-Shield Innovations GmbH<\/a><\/strong><\/td>Austrian MiCAR licence holder<\/td>WhiteBIT EU operating entity for the regulated EEA setup<\/td><\/tr>ATWHITE-Tech GmbH<\/a><\/strong><\/td>Listed shareholder in Austrian company data<\/td>Austrian ownership vehicle of WB-Shield Innovations GmbH<\/td><\/tr>Florian Erlach<\/a><\/strong><\/td>Listed managing director<\/td>Austrian management \/ governance function<\/td><\/tr>Oleksii Chubarov<\/a><\/strong><\/td>Listed managing director<\/td>Austrian management \/ governance function<\/td><\/tr>Michael Fischer<\/a><\/strong><\/td>Listed managing director<\/td>Austrian management \/ governance function<\/td><\/tr><\/tbody><\/table><\/figure>\n\n\n\nAustria As A MiCA Hub<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe WhiteBIT authorisation confirms a broader trend: Austria\u2019s FMA has become a relevant MiCA licensing gateway for both domestic and international crypto firms. This is not merely a local Austrian story. Under MiCAR, a national authorisation can become the basis for EEA-wide service provision, making the choice of regulator strategically important.<\/p>\n\n\n\nAustria has already granted MiCAR authorisations to prominent names such as Bitpanda<\/a><\/strong>, Bybit EU<\/a><\/strong>, and KuCoin EU<\/a><\/strong>, as well as banking, brokerage, and specialist crypto entities. However, the Austrian hub narrative also comes with supervisory pressure. The KuCoin case shows that a licence is not the end of the compliance story: after granting authorisation, the FMA imposed restrictions linked to AML, sanctions and governance key functions, later lifting the new-business ban while still keeping the start of operations restricted due to outstanding supervisory requirements.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThat is the real MiCA lesson: authorisation is only the entry ticket. Ongoing governance is the actual test.<\/p>\n\n\n\nCompliance Assessment<\/h2>\n\n\n\nWhiteBIT\u2019s Austrian authorisation is a positive regulatory milestone and strengthens the company\u2019s European credibility. It also gives Austria another high-profile global crypto player under its supervisory perimeter.<\/p>\n\n\n\nAt the same time, FinTelegram sees three points to monitor:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nService-scope alignment:<\/strong> WhiteBIT EU\u2019s final product offering must match the services actually authorised by the FMA.<\/li>\n\n\n\nCross-border onboarding:<\/strong> Passporting, customer migration, risk disclosures, and legacy-user treatment must be transparent.<\/li>\n\n\n\nGovernance resilience:<\/strong> The Austrian entity must demonstrate sustainable AML, sanctions, compliance, IT-security, and management structures \u2014 not merely documentation at licence date.<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram Working Hypothesis<\/h2>\n\n\n\nWhiteBIT\u2019s MiCA licence is part of a broader regulatory migration by global exchanges seeking a compliant EEA base before the end of the MiCA transitional phase. Austria is emerging as one of the more attractive gateways for serious applicants, but also as a jurisdiction that will be judged by how rigorously it supervises high-profile crypto groups after authorisation.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhistle42 Call For Information<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram invites insiders, former employees, compliance officers, onboarding partners, and affected users with information on WhiteBIT EU, WB-Shield Innovations GmbH, or other FMA-licensed crypto exchanges to share documents, onboarding materials, internal compliance memos, customer-migration notices, or regulatory correspondence via Whistle42.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nShare Information via Whistle42<\/a><\/div>\n<\/div>\n","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"By FinTelegram Editorial Desk The Austrian Financial Market Authority (FMA) has granted a MiCAR authorisation to WB-Shield Innovations GmbH, the Austrian entity operating as WhiteBIT EU. The approval strengthens WhiteBIT\u2019s regulated European footprint and confirms Vienna\u2019s growing role as a MiCA gateway for major crypto platforms. However, the authorised service scope deserves close reading. Investigation Update: Cyprus Service Providers Deny Operational Role As FinTelegram Expands Payment-Rail Review!"},"content":{"rendered":"\nSpread financial intelligence<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nFollowing the publication of FinTelegram\u2019s compliance investigation<\/a> into Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong>, STAKES.com payment-rail allegations<\/strong>, and the broader Cyprus\u2013Cura\u00e7ao casino structure, FinTelegram has received right-of-reply statements from Cyprus-based service providers referenced in the investigation.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe responses came from Dimitris Dimitriou \/ D. Dimitriou & Co LLC<\/a> \/ POLYCON SERVICES LTD<\/a><\/strong> and from McMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd<\/a><\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has updated the original investigation report<\/a> to reflect these clarifications. At the same time, our payment-rail review continues, with a focus on the alleged roles of Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/a><\/strong>, BRB24 TECH N.V.<\/a><\/strong>, STAKES.com<\/a><\/strong>, MiFinity<\/a><\/strong>, Volt.io<\/a><\/strong>, and related payment, merchant, KYB, and transaction infrastructure.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThis update demonstrates the editorial process FinTelegram applies in compliance investigations: allegations are recorded, documentary evidence is reviewed, right-of-reply statements are reflected, and whistleblowers are invited to provide additional material that supports, contradicts, or clarifies the evidence record.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nRight Of Reply Received<\/h2>\n\n\n\nAfter publication, Dimitris Dimitriou<\/strong> stated that neither he personally, nor D. Dimitriou & Co LLC<\/strong>, nor POLYCON SERVICES LTD<\/strong> had any involvement in the operational activities, management, payment processing, commercial affairs, customer transactions, or business decisions of Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong> or any related entity.<\/p>\n\n\n\nHe clarified that the role of POLYCON SERVICES LTD<\/strong> was limited to routine corporate secretarial and administrative services, including filings with the Cyprus Registrar of Companies and the maintenance of corporate records.<\/p>\n\n\n\nMcMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd<\/strong>, through Managing Director Angelo Aristodimou<\/strong>, stated that its role in relation to Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong> was strictly limited to acting as a correspondence address for the submission of documentation to the Cyprus Registrar of Companies.<\/p>\n\n\n\nMcMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd further denied having provided accounting, audit, tax, advisory, restructuring, ownership-decision, payment-processing, customer-transaction, commercial, operational, or business-activity services to Stardust Global CCS Ltd or related entities in connection with the matters discussed in FinTelegram\u2019s investigation.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nFinTelegram Updated The Report<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has updated the original Stardust\/STAKES.com investigation report to include the right-of-reply statements and to make clear that FinTelegram does not<\/strong> allege that McMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd, POLYCON SERVICES LTD, D. Dimitriou & Co LLC, or Dimitris Dimitriou operated STAKES.com, processed player payments, managed customer transactions, or made commercial decisions for Stardust Global CCS Ltd.<\/p>\n\n\n\nTheir relevance in the current investigation is limited to the roles appearing in Cyprus Registrar filings and the associated corporate-services perimeter.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThis distinction is important. FinTelegram\u2019s investigation is not designed to imply operational wrongdoing by every service provider appearing in corporate records. The purpose is to map the infrastructure, identify relevant corporate and payment touchpoints, and determine where responsibility, compliance obligations, and transaction-level evidence may exist.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nThe Payment-Rail Review Continues<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe core of the Stardust investigation remains unchanged.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram is reviewing whistleblower allegations that European player deposits into STAKES.com<\/strong> may have been routed through payment channels involving Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong>, MiFinity<\/strong>, and Volt.io<\/strong>, using e-wallet, open-banking, or related payment infrastructure.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe investigation continues to examine:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nthe corporate structure and ownership history of Stardust Global CCS Ltd;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe transfer of shares from STARSCREAM LIMITED of St. Lucia to BRB24 TECH N.V. of Cura\u00e7ao;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe alleged role of Stardust Global CCS Ltd as a payment, merchant, or corporate layer;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe alleged use of MiFinity, Volt.io, e-wallet, and open-banking channels;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether European players were able to register, deposit, and fund casino accounts through these payment channels;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether merchant onboarding, KYB, AML, or payment-monitoring red flags were identified or ignored;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether the underlying payment descriptors, merchant IDs, settlement records, or transaction references point to Stardust Global CCS Ltd or related entities.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has not yet published a final compliance finding. The investigation remains open.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nWhy This Matters<\/h2>\n\n\n\nUnauthorized or illegal online gambling is not only a player-protection issue. It is also a payment-integrity, AML, and market-transparency issue.<\/p>\n\n\n\nOffshore casino networks often depend on infrastructure that sits around the casino brand itself: corporate agents, payment processors, e-wallets, open-banking gateways, banking partners, legal-service providers, corporate-service firms, domain networks, and technical vendors.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram\u2019s role is to map this infrastructure, distinguish documented facts from allegations, publish right-of-reply statements fairly, and continue asking the central compliance question:<\/p>\n\n\n\nWho enabled the payment flows, who knew what, and what evidence exists at transaction level?<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nWhistleblowers: The Investigation Is Moving<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe immediate responses from Cyprus service providers show that FinTelegram\u2019s staged compliance-investigation model works.<\/p>\n\n\n\nPublication creates movement. Right-of-reply statements clarify roles. Further questions can be asked. New whistleblowers may come forward. The evidence record becomes more precise.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram is now calling again on insiders, payment professionals, compliance officers, affected players, former employees, corporate-service professionals, and regulators to provide information.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWe are particularly seeking:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nbank statements showing deposits to or from STAKES.com;<\/li>\n\n\n\nMiFinity transaction records;<\/li>\n\n\n\nVolt.io payment confirmations or open-banking records;<\/li>\n\n\n\npayment descriptors, merchant names, merchant IDs, settlement references, or transaction IDs;<\/li>\n\n\n\nscreenshots from the STAKES.com cashier showing available deposit methods;<\/li>\n\n\n\nIBANs, beneficiary names, virtual accounts, or PSP reference data;<\/li>\n\n\n\nmerchant agreements involving Stardust Global CCS Ltd, BRB24 TECH N.V., STAKES.com, MiFinity, Volt.io, or intermediaries;<\/li>\n\n\n\nKYB files, UBO declarations, risk assessments, or internal compliance approvals;<\/li>\n\n\n\ncorrespondence with payment providers, banks, casino support, regulators, ombudsman bodies, or corporate-service providers;<\/li>\n\n\n\nevidence that supports, contradicts, corrects, or adds nuance to the allegations.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram is not only looking for incriminating evidence. We are equally interested in exculpatory material, corrections, clarifications, and documents that help establish the accurate scope of each party\u2019s role.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nComing Compliance Intelligence Report<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram is preparing a full Stardust Global \/ STAKES.com Compliance Intelligence Report<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe report will address the Cyprus corporate structure, Cura\u00e7ao and St. Lucia ownership connections, the alleged payment rails, merchant-onboarding and AML questions, player-access evidence, e-wallet and open-banking infrastructure, and the broader role of Cyprus corporate structures in offshore casino operations.<\/p>\n\n\n\nBefore publication of the full report, FinTelegram will continue its editorial verification process and will reflect substantiated right-of-reply statements, clarifications, corrections, and documentary evidence. Whistleblowers, payment insiders, compliance officers, affected players, and service providers: help us map the rails. Releases Technical Correlation Report on the SPTPub Platform Cluster and the Betzter Network"},"content":{"rendered":"\nA new FinTelegram forensic report maps strong technical links between Betzter and a wider casino platform environment through shared telemetry, byte-identical application assets, and direct SPTPub infrastructure markers. The report stops short of alleging common legal ownership, but identifies significant compliance, data-protection, and payment-transparency questions that merit regulatory and provider-side scrutiny.<\/p>\n\n\n\n2-Minutes Briefing<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram is making available for download its new report, \u201cSPTPub Platform Cluster and the Betzter Network: A Technical Correlation Analysis.\u201d<\/strong> The report presents the results of a passive OSINT correlation scan of 17 online casino domains and focuses on reproducible technical links between Betzter, several related casino brands, and a broader SPTPub-linked platform environment.<\/a><\/p>\n\n\n\nThe analysis is based exclusively on public technical artefacts. No login was performed, no account was created, no deposit or payment flow was initiated, and no authentication endpoint or non-public system was accessed during the investigation. Across the completed evidence run, FinTelegram recorded 711 deduplicated technical markers, 55 hashed public assets, 559 certificate-transparency rows, and 133 URLScan references.<\/a><\/p>\n\n\n\nDownload the SPTPub Report here. <\/strong><\/a><\/p>\n\n\n\nKey Findings<\/h2>\n\n\n\n\nBetzter, Aphrodite, Gambiva, MadCasino,<\/strong> and Dracula<\/strong> were found to share core telemetry and deployment markers, including the same FullStory organisation ID, the same Sentry project, the same InvisibleSport<\/strong> and Pusher<\/strong> hosts, and a byte-identical Livewire application bundle.<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\nFour reference domains, namely BCGame, PlayGlobal, Spartibet<\/strong>, and RoxCasino<\/strong>, expose direct SPTPub API<\/strong> or start-host integrations tied to the stable cluster marker\u00a0c7818b61<\/code>, which is consistent with a shared multi-tenant platform structure.<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\nBetzter\u2019s public application layer contains player-linked telemetry markers such as\u00a0Player.uuid<\/code>,\u00a0Player.email<\/code>, private player-account channel patterns, and the event marker\u00a0PlayerAccountUpdatedBalance<\/code>, indicating a detailed player-event and telemetry architecture.<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\nBetzter\u2019s recovered Sentry Replay<\/strong> configuration showed\u00a0maskAllInputs false<\/code>,\u00a0maskAllText false<\/code>, and\u00a0blockAllMedia false<\/code>, which raises material questions about session replay, identity-linked profiling, and GDPR-grade data governance.<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\nThe endpoint\u00a0api.sptpub.com<\/code>\u00a0was observed resolving to\u00a0159.69.41.140<\/code>\u00a0and\u00a0159.69.41.141<\/code>\u00a0in Hetzner AS24940, making it the strongest current direct non-Cloudflare infrastructure lead in the dataset.<\/li>\n\n\n\nDespite extensive public checks, no confirmed cashier provider, PSP, merchant ID, gateway ID, connector ID, route UUID, acquirer, or Apple Pay merchant association was identified for Betzter, leaving the payment route as a documented and highly relevant disclosure gap.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nCompact report description<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe report identifies technical risk indicators<\/strong>, not proven wrongdoing. It establishes a strong technical relationship between several named domains through shared telemetry identifiers, direct platform markers, and byte-identical public assets, but it does not<\/strong> establish common legal ownership, beneficial ownership, or any regulatory breach as a matter of law.<\/a><\/p>\n\n\n\nFrom a compliance perspective, the relevance is clear. The findings raise questions about cross-brand telemetry aggregation, session replay, player profiling, opaque platform tenancy, and undisclosed payment-routing structures, all of which are directly relevant to regulators, payment institutions, hosting providers, and data-protection authorities.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe most decisive next evidence is likely held by providers rather than by further passive scanning. According to the report, the key disclosure targets are SPTPub, FullStory, Sentry, Pusher, Hetzner, and any payment or cashier providers that hold tenant records, account ownership data, routing metadata, and settlement information.<\/a><\/p>\n\n\n\nWhistleblower call<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram encourages whistleblowers with additional information on SPTPub, Betzter, Softon Ltd, InvisibleSport, NovaFlick, related casino brands, platform tenancy, payment routing, PSP relationships, acquirers, telemetry ownership, or internal compliance concerns<\/strong> to submit material securely via Whistle42<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nParticularly valuable are internal contracts, onboarding records, merchant-routing data, tenant and Brand ID mappings, PSP and acquirer correspondence, telemetry account ownership records, hosting-provider disclosures, and communications with regulators or service providers.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nShare Information via Whistle42<\/a><\/div>\n<\/div>\n","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"A new FinTelegram forensic report maps strong technical links between Betzter and a wider casino platform environment through shared telemetry, byte-identical application assets, and direct SPTPub infrastructure markers. sk\/","title":{"rendered":"Compliance Alert: Binance\u2019s Greek MiCA Gamble Reportedly Fails \u2014 EU Access Turns Into A Wind-Down Test"},"content":{"rendered":"\nBinance<\/strong>\u2019s European MiCA strategy is now in crisis mode. According to Reuters<\/a> and Bloomberg Law<\/a>, the Greek regulator Hellenic Capital Market Commission (HCMC)<\/strong> is unlikely to approve Binance\u2019s MiCA licence application. If confirmed, the world\u2019s largest crypto exchange would lose the legal basis to serve EU clients from 1 July 2026<\/strong>, unless it can present another authorised MiCA path before the deadline.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThis is not a routine licensing delay. It is the first major stress test for the EU\u2019s new crypto regime. MiCA is designed to end the old model of fragmented national registrations, regulatory shopping, and offshore group structures serving EU clients through loose legal gateways. Binance appears to have bet on Greece as its EU passporting hub. That bet is now turning into a regulatory cliff edge.<\/p>\n\n\n\nKey Facts<\/h2>\n\n\n\nItem<\/th>Finding<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>Applicant<\/td>Binance, reportedly through its Greek structure connected to Binary Greece<\/strong><\/td><\/tr>Regulator<\/td>Hellenic Capital Market Commission (HCMC)<\/strong><\/td><\/tr>Regime<\/td>EU Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA<\/strong>)<\/td><\/tr>Deadline<\/td>30 June 2026<\/strong> for authorisation; hard stop from 1 July 2026<\/strong><\/td><\/tr>Current status<\/td>Reported rejection \/ no public approval<\/td><\/tr>Binance position<\/td>Application allegedly considered compliant; update promised before 30 June<\/td><\/tr>Compliance impact<\/td>Without MiCA authorisation, Binance cannot continue normal EU client services<\/td><\/tr>FinTelegram risk rating<\/td>RED \u2014 EU Authorisation Failure \/ Client Migration Risk<\/strong><\/td><\/tr><\/tbody><\/table><\/figure>\n\n\n\nThe MiCA Cliff<\/h2>\n\n\n\nMiCA creates a simple legal outcome: a crypto-asset service provider either has an authorised EU CASP licence and can passport its services across the EU, or it must stop serving EU clients. The transitional period ends on 1 July 2026. There is no informal grace period for a global exchange that is still \u201cin dialogue\u201d with regulators.<\/p>\n\n\n\nESMA has already told the market what must happen: unlicensed CASPs must have credible, executable wind-down plans; client assets must be protected; client migration must be controlled; and unauthorised third-country entities cannot continue business-as-usual through reverse solicitation narratives or group outsourcing structures.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFor Binance, this creates an immediate operational question: Which authorised EU legal entity, if any, will serve its EU users after 30 June?<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nWhy Greece Matters<\/h2>\n\n\n\nBinance selected Greece as its MiCA home base rather than a larger or more established crypto supervisory jurisdiction. That choice was unconventional. Greece had not been among the leading MiCA authorisation centres, while competitors had already secured licences in jurisdictions such as Luxembourg, Ireland, Malta, and Austria.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram\u2019s interpretation: Binance\u2019s Greek route looked like a strategic licensing shortcut. The reported rejection suggests that EU regulators are not prepared to treat MiCA as a passport-shopping exercise for high-risk global platforms.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe Greek decision, if confirmed, is therefore larger than Binance. It sends a message to the market: MiCA authorisation is not a branding exercise. It is a supervisory gate.<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nThe Binance Legacy Problem<\/h2>\n\n\n\nBinance is not a clean applicant. Its compliance history is part of the regulatory file.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe group previously resolved major U.S. criminal, AML, and sanctions violations. Its founder Changpeng Zhao stepped down as CEO after pleading guilty to U.S. Bank Secrecy Act violations. The U.S. settlement imposed massive penalties and a compliance monitorship.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThat history matters under MiCA. A European CASP authorisation is not only about systems, policies, and capital. It is about governance, ownership, fitness and propriety, AML controls, outsourcing, custody arrangements, conflicts of interest, sanctions screening, and the real ability of supervisors to control the EU-facing business.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFor a global exchange with complex group structures, offshore operations, multiple product lines, and a history of regulatory clashes, the burden is heavy. Binance may have expanded its compliance team and infrastructure, but MiCA regulators are not required to accept a transformation narrative at face value.<\/p>\n\n\n\nCompetitive Impact<\/h2>\n\n\n\nIf Binance loses EU access, the immediate winners are licensed competitors. Coinbase, Kraken, OKX, Bybit and other MiCA-authorised platforms will benefit from forced client migration, liquidity redistribution, and institutional preference for regulated venues.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThis is the moment MiCA changes the competitive map. The EU crypto market moves from scale-based dominance to licence-based legitimacy.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram Assessment<\/h2>\n\n\n\nBinance is facing a decisive European compliance test. The issue is not whether Binance is large, liquid, popular, or technologically capable. The issue is whether it can pass the EU\u2019s new gatekeeping regime for crypto service providers.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIf the Greek rejection is confirmed, Binance will have three realistic options:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nstop serving EU clients;<\/li>\n\n\n\nmigrate EU users to an authorised CASP structure;<\/li>\n\n\n\nattempt a last-minute alternative licensing route, which appears operationally and procedurally weak given the deadline.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram\u2019s bottom line: No MiCA licence, no EU business.<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nThe old crypto playbook \u2014 global platform, offshore structures, national registrations, and aggressive market access \u2014 is over in Europe. Binance is now the test case for whether MiCA will be enforced as a real supervisory regime or diluted into another regulatory passport market.<\/p>\n\n\n\nCompliance Rating<\/h2>\n\n\n\nRED \u2014 High Regulatory Risk<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nReason: no publicly confirmed MiCA authorisation, reported rejection risk in Greece, hard EU cut-off on 1 July 2026, unresolved client migration exposure, and legacy AML\/sanctions risk.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram Call For Information<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram invites Binance users, former employees, compliance staff, payment partners, banking partners, and regulatory insiders to provide information on:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nBinance\u2019s EU client migration plans;<\/li>\n\n\n\ninternal communications regarding the Greek MiCA application;<\/li>\n\n\n\ninstructions given to EU users;<\/li>\n\n\n\npayment, custody, and fiat withdrawal arrangements;<\/li>\n\n\n\nany alternative licensing or white-label arrangements;<\/li>\n\n\n\nregulatory communications with HCMC, ESMA, AMF, BaFin, CONSOB, CNMV or other EU regulators.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nInformation can be submitted confidentially via Whistle42.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nShare Information via Whistle42<\/a><\/div>\n<\/div>\n","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"Binance\u2019s EU strategy is under pressure. 8T10:14:36","modified_gmt":"2026-06-18T10:14:36","slug":"fintelegram-investigation-stardust-global-ccs-ltd-and-stakes-com-payment-rail-allegations-whistleblowers-sought","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/fintelegram-investigation-stardust-global-ccs-ltd-and-stakes-com-payment-rail-allegations-whistleblowers-sought\/","title":{"rendered":"FinTelegram Investigation: Stardust Global CCS Ltd and STAKES.com Payment-Rail Allegations \u2014 Whistleblowers Sought"},"content":{"rendered":"\nUpdate \u2014 Right of Reply from Cyprus Service Providers<\/h3>\n\n\n\nFollowing publication, FinTelegram received responses from Dimitris Dimitriou \/ D. Dimitriou & Co LLC \/ POLYCON SERVICES LTD<\/strong> and from McMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd<\/strong>.<\/em><\/p>\n\n\n\nDimitris Dimitriou \/ D. Dimitriou & Co LLC \/ POLYCON SERVICES LTD<\/strong>, in a further response, reiterated that neither he personally, nor D. Dimitriou & Co LLC, nor POLYCON SERVICES LTD had or have any involvement in the operational activities, management, payment processing, customer transactions, commercial affairs, business model, licensing matters, or business decisions of Stardust Global CCS Ltd or any related entity. He stated that such matters fall within the responsibility of the company\u2019s directors or authorised representatives. Dimitriou further clarified that, as a regulated law firm and corporate services provider, customer due diligence and ongoing monitoring procedures are applied as part of the firm\u2019s compliance framework, and that beneficial ownership information and statutory records are maintained and updated as required by applicable law. He stressed that these are legal and administrative functions and should not be construed as involvement in, oversight of, or responsibility for the operational activities of a client. FinTelegram records this clarification and does not allege any operational or payment-processing role by Dimitris Dimitriou, D. Dimitriou & Co LLC, or POLYCON SERVICES LTD.<\/em><\/p>\n\n\n\nMcMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd<\/strong>, through Managing Director Angelo Aristodimou<\/strong>, stated that its sole involvement in relation to Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong> was limited to the incorporation of the company through an intermediary agent and the provision of a registered correspondence address for filing purposes with the Cyprus Registrar of Companies. McMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd further stated that it conducted standard client acceptance and KYC procedures in accordance with applicable legal and regulatory requirements and had no further involvement with the company or its affairs following the incorporation process. The firm denies having acted as accountant, auditor, tax adviser, company secretary, payment processor, manager, director, shareholder, beneficial owner, nominee, consultant, or operational service provider to Stardust Global CCS Ltd. It also denies any involvement in the company\u2019s business activities, operations, management, ownership decisions, customer transactions, payment flows, online gambling activities, STAKES.com, or any matters referred to in this publication. FinTelegram records this clarification and does not allege that McMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd had any operational or payment-processing role in relation to Stardust Global CCS Ltd or STAKES.com.<\/em><\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram notes these clarifications. Our investigation does not allege that McMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd, POLYCON SERVICES LTD, D. Dimitriou & Co LLC or Dimitris Dimitriou operated STAKES.com, processed player payments, managed customer transactions, or made commercial decisions for Stardust Global CCS Ltd. Their relevance in this investigation is currently limited to the roles appearing in Cyprus Registrar filings and the associated corporate-services perimeter.<\/em><\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nSpread financial intelligence<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has opened a formal compliance investigation into Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong>, a Cyprus-registered company that appears in corporate filings connected to offshore structures and alleged online casino payment flows involving STAKES.com<\/a><\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWe have received a detailed whistleblower submission, Cyprus corporate registry material, and preliminary payment-rail allegations concerning BRB24 TECH N.V.<\/strong>, STAKES.com<\/strong>, MiFinity<\/strong>, Volt.io<\/strong>, and a Cyprus-based corporate-services layer involving McMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd<\/strong>, POLYCON SERVICES LTD<\/strong>, and related legal-service providers.<\/p>\n\n\n\nAt this stage, FinTelegram is not publishing a final compliance finding. We are opening a structured investigation and are calling on whistleblowers, insiders, affected players, payment professionals, compliance officers, corporate-service providers, and regulators to assist with additional evidence.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFor the avoidance of doubt: this investigation concerns STAKES.com<\/strong> and the entities and payment structures described below. It should not be confused with similarly named brands or operators unless expressly referenced in verified evidence.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nWe Are Investigating<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram\u2019s EU iGaming Payments Desk is reviewing whether Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong> functioned as part of a cross-border payment or corporate-services layer for offshore casino activity targeting consumers in regulated European jurisdictions.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe investigation focuses on the following areas:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nthe corporate structure and ownership history of Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong> in Cyprus;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe documented transfer of shares from STARSCREAM LIMITED<\/strong> of St. Lucia to BRB24 TECH N.V.<\/strong> of Cura\u00e7ao;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe role of Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong> as an alleged payment or merchant layer connected to STAKES.com<\/strong>;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe appearances of Cyprus-based professional-service providers in Registrar filings, including correspondence, secretarial, legal, and administrative references, and the limits of those roles;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe alleged use of EU payment infrastructure, e-wallets, and open-banking rails involving MiFinity<\/strong> and Volt.io<\/strong>;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether European players were able to register, fund accounts, and deposit through payment channels despite local licensing requirements;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether payment providers or other relevant intermediaries received compliance red flags and how they responded; and, where applicable, what KYB or client-acceptance checks were performed by corporate-service providers within their limited administrative roles.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nThe core question is whether a Cyprus corporate vehicle and associated professional-service infrastructure were used as a regulatory arbitrage layer between offshore casino operations and European player funds.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nWhat The Cyprus Filings Indicate So Far<\/h2>\n\n\n\nCyprus corporate filings reviewed by FinTelegram indicate that Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong> was incorporated in Cyprus in 2023. The filings show a structure involving offshore and professional-service entities.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe documents reviewed by FinTelegram indicate:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nSTARSCREAM LIMITED<\/strong>, a St. Lucia entity, appears in the shareholder history of Stardust Global CCS Ltd;<\/li>\n\n\n\nin September 2024, shares in Stardust Global CCS Ltd were transferred to BRB24 TECH N.V.<\/strong>, a Cura\u00e7ao entity;<\/li>\n\n\n\nSTARKEAST MANAGEMENT B.V.<\/strong>, with Cura\u00e7ao address details, appears in early filings as a director;<\/li>\n\n\n\nPOLYCON SERVICES LTD<\/strong> appears as corporate secretary;<\/li>\n\n\n\nMcMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd<\/strong> appears as correspondence contact in several filings;<\/li>\n\n\n\nin 2026, STARKEAST MANAGEMENT B.V.<\/strong> was replaced by SOTU26 LTD<\/strong>, a Nevis entity, as director.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nThese corporate records do not, by themselves, prove wrongdoing. However, they raise legitimate compliance questions when viewed together with the whistleblower allegations concerning online casino deposits, EU payment rails, and offshore licensing structures.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nPayment-Rail Allegations Under Review<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe whistleblower submission alleges that European player deposits into STAKES.com<\/strong> were routed through payment channels involving Stardust Global CCS Ltd<\/strong>, MiFinity<\/strong>, and Volt.io<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nAccording to the whistleblower, payment flows may have involved a combination of:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\na Cyprus corporate or merchant layer;<\/li>\n\n\n\ne-wallet funding or transfer functionality;<\/li>\n\n\n\nopen-banking payment infrastructure;<\/li>\n\n\n\ncasino cashier interfaces;<\/li>\n\n\n\npayment descriptors or merchant records allegedly connected to Stardust Global CCS Ltd or associated entities.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has not yet completed its verification of these alleged payment flows. We are therefore requesting additional evidence from affected users, payment insiders, compliance officers, former employees, banking partners, open-banking providers, and corporate-service professionals.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nTransparency Matters!<\/h2>\n\n\n\nIllegal or unauthorized online gambling is not only a consumer-protection issue. It is also a payment-integrity and AML issue. Where offshore casino operators accept deposits from consumers in regulated European markets without appropriate local authorization, payment providers and intermediaries may face serious compliance questions, including:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nmerchant onboarding and KYB failures;<\/li>\n\n\n\nweak monitoring of high-risk gambling merchants;<\/li>\n\n\n\nimproper reliance on offshore licenses;<\/li>\n\n\n\nfailure to detect or stop transactions linked to unauthorized gambling;<\/li>\n\n\n\nuse of corporate-service structures to obscure the true operating model;<\/li>\n\n\n\ninadequate responses to consumer complaints and AML red flags.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has repeatedly observed that offshore casino networks often depend less on the casino website itself than on the surrounding infrastructure: corporate agents, payment processors, open-banking gateways, wallets, legal-service providers, domain networks, and technical vendors.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThat infrastructure is what we are now mapping.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nWe Need More Evidence<\/h2>\n\n\n\nInvestigations of this kind depend on documents and first-hand accounts that cannot be obtained from public registries alone. FinTelegram is specifically looking for the following material:<\/p>\n\n\n\n1. Payment-Flow Evidence<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\nbank statements showing deposits to or from STAKES.com;<\/li>\n\n\n\nMiFinity transaction records;<\/li>\n\n\n\nVolt.io payment confirmations or open-banking records;<\/li>\n\n\n\npayment descriptors, merchant names, merchant IDs, settlement references, or transaction IDs;<\/li>\n\n\n\nscreenshots from the STAKES.com cashier showing available deposit methods;<\/li>\n\n\n\nIBANs, beneficiary names, virtual accounts, or PSP reference data;<\/li>\n\n\n\nrefund, chargeback, failed-payment, or withdrawal records.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\n2. Merchant Onboarding and KYB Material<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\nmerchant agreements involving Stardust Global CCS Ltd, BRB24 TECH N.V., STAKES.com, MiFinity, Volt.io, or intermediaries;<\/li>\n\n\n\nKYB files, UBO declarations, risk assessments, or internal compliance approvals;<\/li>\n\n\n\nlicensing representations made to banks, payment institutions, e-wallet providers, or open-banking platforms;<\/li>\n\n\n\ndocuments describing the role of Stardust Global CCS Ltd as merchant, payment agent, payment facilitator, nominee, or corporate services vehicle.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\n3. Internal Compliance and Legal Communications<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\nAML red-flag assessments;<\/li>\n\n\n\ninternal warnings or escalations concerning STAKES.com or Stardust Global CCS Ltd;<\/li>\n\n\n\ncorrespondence with McMillan Woods Cyprus Ltd, POLYCON SERVICES LTD, D. DIMITRIOU & CO LLC, BRB24 TECH N.V., STARSCREAM LIMITED, STARKEAST MANAGEMENT B.V., SOTU26 LTD, MiFinity, Volt.io, or related parties;<\/li>\n\n\n\nboard minutes, legal opinions, or onboarding memoranda;<\/li>\n\n\n\ncommunications discussing EU player traffic, geoblocking, licensing, or payment restrictions.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\n4. Player and Consumer-Complaint Evidence<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\nSTAKES.com account records;<\/li>\n\n\n\nregistration screenshots showing EU or UK residence acceptance;<\/li>\n\n\n\nlive-chat transcripts;<\/li>\n\n\n\nKYC requests;<\/li>\n\n\n\ncomplaint correspondence with MiFinity, Volt.io, banks, regulators, ombudsman bodies, or casino support;<\/li>\n\n\n\naccount closures, blocked accounts, or refusal-to-refund communications after complaints were filed.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\n5. Contradictory or Exculpatory Evidence<\/h3>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram is not only looking for incriminating evidence. We also want information that corrects, contradicts, or adds nuance to the allegations.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIf you represent or have worked with any of the named entities and believe the whistleblower allegations are inaccurate, incomplete, or misleading, we want to hear from you as well.<\/p>\n\n\n\nEntity & Role Map \u2014 Stardust Global \/ STAKES.com Compliance Investigation<\/h2>\n\n\n\nName<\/th>Type<\/th>Domain \/ Identifier<\/th>Jurisdiction<\/th>Role \/ Relevance<\/th>Current Evidence Status<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>STAKES.com<\/strong><\/td>Brand \/ Casino Platform<\/td>stakes.com<\/code><\/td>Under review; allegedly offshore \/ Cura\u00e7ao-linked<\/td>Online casino brand at the center of the whistleblower allegations. Compliance Report: Cyprus Structures, Offshore Casino Narratives, EU Payment Exposure And Technical Risk Signals"},"content":{"rendered":"\nFinTelegram has published a new Compliance Intelligence Report<\/strong> on Softon Ltd<\/a><\/strong>, the Cyprus-registered company publicly identified<\/a> by Betzter.com<\/a><\/strong> as its owner and operator. The report is now available for download and provides a structured review of a broader risk environment involving Cyprus corporate structures, offshore gambling narratives, EU-facing activity indicators, payment-route opacity, technical infrastructure, and player-data telemetry.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe central finding is not a simple \u201cone casino, one operator\u201d story. The report maps a layered compliance picture around Softon Ltd<\/strong>, Betzter<\/strong>, KingdomCasino<\/a><\/strong>, SPTPub<\/a><\/strong>, InvisibleSport<\/a><\/strong>, NovaFlick<\/a><\/strong>, Transferop<\/a><\/strong>, and related infrastructure or payment indicators. The report does not allege proven criminal conduct by any named person or entity. It raises serious compliance questions that regulators, payment providers, banks, hosting providers, and data-protection authorities should examine.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe Softon Layer: Cyprus Company, Offshore Casino Narrative, EU-Facing Risk<\/h2>\n\n\n\nAccording to the documented materials reviewed by FinTelegram, Softon Ltd<\/strong> was incorporated in Cyprus on 9 August 2024<\/strong> with a share capital of EUR 2,000<\/strong>. The company is publicly named by Betzter.com<\/strong> as the platform\u2019s owner and operator.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe report examines whether this Cyprus corporate layer is part of a broader online casino and payment environment that may involve:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nan Anjouan licence narrative<\/strong> for gambling operations;<\/li>\n\n\n\nEU-facing accessibility and market indicators;<\/li>\n\n\n\npossible reliance on EU payment rails;<\/li>\n\n\n\nlinks or references to Kaspela Labyrinth O\u00dc<\/a><\/strong> in Estonia;<\/li>\n\n\n\nunresolved questions around cashier infrastructure, merchant routing, acquirer chains, settlement beneficiaries, and payment-service providers.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nThe report\u2019s most important payment finding is not that a final PSP chain has been proven. Rather, the report shows that key payment infrastructure questions remain opaque<\/strong> at precisely the points where transparency is most important: cashier provider, merchant ID, gateway identifiers, PSP route, acquirer chain, and settlement beneficiary.<\/p>\n\n\n\nTechnical Findings: Player Telemetry, Application-Layer Signals And German Hosting Lead<\/h2>\n\n\n\n<\/figure>\n<\/div>\n\n\nThe report also highlights technical indicators that should matter to gambling regulators, AML supervisors, payment institutions, hosting providers, and data-protection authorities.<\/p>\n\n\n\nPublicly accessible application-layer indicators reviewed in the investigation point to a real-time player-account architecture and telemetry configurations involving tools such as FullStory<\/strong>, Sentry Replay<\/strong>, Pusher<\/strong>, and player-linked fields including Player.uuid<\/strong> and Player.email<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThese indicators create a potentially significant privacy and compliance risk surface. If player-linked identifiers and behavioural telemetry are processed in connection with an offshore casino environment, the relevant questions include:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nwho controls the player data;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhere the data is stored and processed;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether EU players are involved;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether privacy disclosures are adequate;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether session replay or telemetry tools capture sensitive account or payment-related information;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether service providers have been properly informed of the gambling and compliance context.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nThe strongest direct infrastructure lead currently identified in the report is the api.sptpub.com<\/strong> endpoint resolving into Hetzner-hosted IP space in Germany<\/strong>. This makes the case relevant not only for gambling and AML supervisors, but also for infrastructure and hosting-provider compliance teams.<\/p>\n\n\n\nMethodology: Whistleblower Material, OSINT, Technical Review And AI-Assisted Structuring<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe Softon Compliance Report was built from multiple intelligence layers:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nwhistleblower submissions;<\/li>\n\n\n\ncorporate-registry analysis;<\/li>\n\n\n\npassive OSINT collection;<\/li>\n\n\n\napplication-layer and infrastructure review;<\/li>\n\n\n\ntechnical correlation work;<\/li>\n\n\n\nstructured evidentiary grading;<\/li>\n\n\n\nAI-assisted organisation and cross-checking of findings.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram applied its usual evidence framework and separated confirmed facts<\/strong>, strong indicators<\/strong>, investigative leads<\/strong>, and unresolved questions<\/strong>. This distinction is important. The purpose of the report is not to overstate attribution, but to identify the compliance issues that require further scrutiny.<\/p>\n\n\n\nA specialised network and systems expert contributed to the technical review of the infrastructure and application-layer findings. This contribution helped ensure that the technical section remained anchored in reproducible artefacts, reasonable evidentiary boundaries, and clearly defined disclosure targets for providers, payment institutions, regulators, and infrastructure operators.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhy This Report Matters<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe Softon case is a good example of how modern offshore casino structures may operate across multiple layers:<\/p>\n\n\n\nLayer<\/th>Compliance Relevance<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>Cyprus company layer<\/strong><\/td>Softon Ltd as publicly identified operator \/ owner of Betzter<\/td><\/tr>Offshore licence narrative<\/strong><\/td>Anjouan licence context and regulatory-perimeter questions<\/td><\/tr>EU-facing indicators<\/strong><\/td>Potential exposure to EU players, regulators, PSPs and data-protection rules<\/td><\/tr>Payment opacity<\/strong><\/td>Cashier, PSP route, MID, acquirer chain and settlement beneficiary not publicly transparent<\/td><\/tr>Technical infrastructure<\/strong><\/td>api.sptpub.com and Hetzner-hosted IP space as relevant infrastructure lead<\/td><\/tr>Player telemetry<\/strong><\/td>FullStory, Sentry Replay, Pusher and player-linked fields create privacy and compliance questions<\/td><\/tr>Whistleblower input<\/strong><\/td>Source material points to broader casino and payment structures requiring verification<\/td><\/tr><\/tbody><\/table><\/figure>\n\n\n\nThis is exactly the type of structure that often remains invisible when regulators, banks, payment schemes and infrastructure providers review only one domain, one corporate entity, or one payment screenshot in isolation.<\/p>\n\n\n\nNo Final Allegation \u2014 But A Serious Compliance Warning<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram emphasises that the report should not be read as a final finding of criminal conduct by any named person or entity. It should be read as a compliance warning<\/strong> and a call for scrutiny<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe documented combination of a Cyprus corporate vehicle, offshore gambling narrative, EU-facing indicators, application-layer telemetry exposure, hosting infrastructure in Germany, and opaque payment routing deserves immediate attention from:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\ngambling regulators;<\/li>\n\n\n\nAML supervisors;<\/li>\n\n\n\npayment institutions and acquiring banks;<\/li>\n\n\n\nhosting and infrastructure providers;<\/li>\n\n\n\ndata-protection authorities;<\/li>\n\n\n\ncompliance teams at technology and telemetry vendors;<\/li>\n\n\n\nconsumer-protection bodies.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nDownload The Report<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe full Softon Compliance Intelligence Report<\/strong> is available for download here:<\/p>\n\n\n\nDownload the Softon Compliance Report<\/a><\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nCall For Information<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram invites whistleblowers, former employees, players, affiliates, payment insiders, PSP compliance officers, hosting providers, technical service providers, and regulators to provide verifiable information on:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nSofton Ltd<\/strong>;<\/li>\n\n\n\nBetzter<\/strong>;<\/li>\n\n\n\nKingdomCasino<\/strong>;<\/li>\n\n\n\nSPTPub<\/strong>;<\/li>\n\n\n\nInvisibleSport<\/strong>;<\/li>\n\n\n\nNovaFlick<\/strong>;<\/li>\n\n\n\nTransferop<\/strong>;<\/li>\n\n\n\nCyprus-linked casino and payment structures;<\/li>\n\n\n\ncashier providers, PSPs, MIDs, gateways, acquirers and settlement beneficiaries;<\/li>\n\n\n\nKYC\/KYB files, merchant records, contracts, platform-account records and compliance memoranda;<\/li>\n\n\n\ntechnical documentation, telemetry configurations, hosting records and provider communications.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram protects confidential sources and evaluates all credible submissions in the public interest. Whistleblower-derived claims are handled under strict source-protection standards and clearly distinguished from verified findings, strong indicators, and investigative leads.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nShare Information via Whistle42<\/a><\/div>\n<\/div>\n","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"FinTelegram has published a downloadable Compliance Intelligence Report on Softon Ltd, the Cyprus-registered company publicly identified by Betzter.com as its owner and operator. Ltd","slug":"softon-ltd","taxonomy":"post_tag","_links":{"self":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/tags\/16902","targetHints":{"allow":["GET"]}}],"collection":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/tags"}],"about":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/taxonomies\/post_tag"}],"wp:post_type":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/posts?tags=16902"}],"curies":[{"name":"wp","href":"https:\/\/api.w.org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}},{"id":16921,"link":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/tag\/sptpub\/","name":"SPTPub","slug":"sptpub","taxonomy":"post_tag","_links":{"self":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/tags\/16921","targetHints":{"allow":["GET"]}}],"collection":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/tags"}],"about":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/taxonomies\/post_tag"}],"wp:post_type":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/posts?tags=16921"}],"curies":[{"name":"wp","href":"https:\/\/api.w.org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}},{"id":16635,"link":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/tag\/transferop\/","name":"Transferop","slug":"transferop","taxonomy":"post_tag","_links":{"self":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/tags\/16635","targetHints":{"allow":["GET"]}}],"collection":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/tags"}],"about":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/taxonomies\/post_tag"}],"wp:post_type":[{"href":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/posts?tags=16635"}],"curies":[{"name":"wp","href":"https:\/\/api.w.org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}}]]}},{"id":81477,"date":"2026-06-15T14:17:31","date_gmt":"2026-06-15T14:17:31","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/?p=81477"},"modified":"2026-06-15T14:17:34","modified_gmt":"2026-06-15T14:17:34","slug":"latvijas-banka-licenses-fibonatix-high-risk-payment-processor","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/fintelegram.com\/latvijas-banka-licenses-fibonatix-high-risk-payment-processor\/","title":{"rendered":"Compliance Radar: Latvijas Banka Licenses Fibonatix \u2014 A Fresh EU Licence For A High-Risk Payment Legacy?"},"content":{"rendered":"\nLatvijas Banka has issued a payment<\/a><\/strong> institution licence to SIA Fibonatix (LV)<\/strong>, authorising the company to provide payment acceptance and acquiring services for merchants. On paper, this is another fintech licensing success story for Latvia. From FinTelegram\u2019s compliance perspective, however, the decision deserves a much closer look.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has monitored Fibonatix<\/strong> for years as a high-risk payment processor<\/strong> with historical exposure to the binary options and broker-scam era. Earlier FinTelegram reports linked Fibonatix to payment flows around the Yukom\/BinaryBook<\/strong> binary options fraud case and later to the ecosystem around Gal Barak\u2019s E&G Bulgaria<\/strong> and related high-risk broker structures. A new EU licence does not erase that legacy. It increases the supervisory relevance of the legacy.<\/p>\n\n\n\nKey Data<\/h2>\n\n\n\nItem<\/th>Information<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>Licensed entity<\/td>SIA Fibonatix (LV)<\/strong><\/td><\/tr>Regulator<\/td>Latvijas Banka<\/strong><\/td><\/tr>Licence status<\/td>Authorized Payment Institution<\/strong><\/td><\/tr>Licence No.<\/td>27-55\/2026\/12<\/strong><\/td><\/tr>Valid from<\/td>11 June 2026<\/strong><\/td><\/tr>Permitted activity<\/td>Acquiring of payment transactions \/ payment acceptance services<\/strong><\/td><\/tr>Target clients<\/td>high-risk Merchants<\/strong><\/td><\/tr>Known group footprint<\/td>Fibonatix has historically operated through UK, German and Israeli-linked structures<\/td><\/tr>FinTelegram status<\/td>High-Risk Legacy Processor \u2014 Under Monitoring<\/strong><\/td><\/tr><\/tbody><\/table><\/figure>\n\n\n\nThe Licensing Event<\/h2>\n\n\n\nAccording to Latvijas Banka, its Supervision Committee issued the licence to SIA Fibonatix (LV)<\/strong> on 11 June 2026. The licence allows the Latvian entity to provide payment acceptance services to merchants. In the official register, the entity is listed as an authorised payment institution with permission for acquiring of payment transactions.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThis is a meaningful development. Payment acquiring is a critical chokepoint in the financial system. It determines which merchants can accept card and other electronic payments, how risk is monitored, how chargebacks are handled, and how suspicious transaction patterns are detected or ignored.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFor a normal low-risk merchant acquirer, the licence would be a routine fintech item. For Fibonatix<\/strong>, the historical context makes it a compliance story.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe FinTelegram Background<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram first reported on Fibonatix in the context of the Yukom Enterprise<\/strong> binary options fraud case. The FinTelegram money-flow analysis identified payment service providers and facilitators that allegedly enabled deposits into brands such as BinaryBook<\/strong>, BigOption<\/strong>, and BinaryOnline<\/strong>. Fibonatix appeared in that context as one of the processors around the payment infrastructure used by the scheme.<\/p>\n\n\n\nRead our Fibonatix reports here.<\/a> <\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram later reported that Fibonatix had been active in high-risk verticals such as binary options, forex, gambling, gaming, and cryptocurrencies. In the December 2021 and September 2022 updates, FinTelegram described Fibonatix as a notorious high-risk payment processor and referred to its exposure to Yukom<\/strong>, E&G Bulgaria<\/strong>, Tradotrax<\/strong>, Alpha Capital House<\/strong>, and other broker-scam environments.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThese reports do not mean that the newly licensed Latvian entity has committed wrongdoing. They do, however, form part of the relevant compliance history. A regulator assessing fitness, governance, AML controls, merchant onboarding, and risk appetite should not treat this history as irrelevant.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe Core Compliance Question<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe central question is not whether Fibonatix can be licensed. Latvijas Banka has made that decision. The question is whether a payment group with such a high-risk legacy can now demonstrate a materially different governance, compliance, onboarding, monitoring, and reporting culture.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFor high-risk processors, the critical test is not the licence itself. The critical test is the merchant book after licensing.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram would expect particular attention to the following areas:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nMerchant onboarding controls<\/strong>Which merchant verticals will Fibonatix (LV) accept? Will it process for gaming, gambling, crypto, forex, investment platforms, lead-generation networks, affiliate-driven offers, or other high-risk merchants?<\/li>\n\n\n\nLegacy and related-party review<\/strong>Did the licensing process include a structured review of the historical Fibonatix group activities, including its binary options-era exposure and prior FinTelegram reports?<\/li>\n\n\n\nCross-border acquiring model<\/strong>Will the Latvian licence be used mainly for Latvian merchants, or as a broader EU acquiring hub for international high-risk merchants?<\/li>\n\n\n\nTransaction monitoring and chargeback intelligence<\/strong>How will Fibonatix detect fraud patterns, refund abuse, victim complaints, mismatched merchant descriptors, and suspicious chargeback spikes?<\/li>\n\n\n\nRegulatory cooperation<\/strong>Given the prior UK, German and Israeli-linked footprint of the Fibonatix brand, will Latvijas Banka coordinate with other supervisory authorities where relevant?<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\nWhy This Matters<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe binary options era showed that payment processors were not neutral bystanders. In many broker-scam cases, the processors were the operational gatekeepers that allowed fraudulent platforms to collect money from victims across borders. Without payment access, many of these schemes could not have scaled.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThat is why FinTelegram has long argued that payment rails are accountability rails<\/strong>. Licensing a historically high-risk payment processor creates a public-interest question: has the business genuinely changed, or has it simply moved into a new regulated jurisdiction?<\/p>\n\n\n\nLatvia has positioned itself as a fintech-friendly jurisdiction. That strategy can be legitimate and economically useful. But fintech licensing must not become regulatory rehabilitation without public transparency. A licence should be the beginning of enhanced scrutiny, not the end of the discussion.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram Assessment<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram classifies the Latvian licensing of SIA Fibonatix (LV)<\/strong> as a Compliance Watch event<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe licence may represent a legitimate new regulatory chapter for Fibonatix. However, given the historical FinTelegram reporting, the binary options-era exposure, and the high-risk merchant acquiring context, the case deserves close monitoring by regulators, banks, card schemes, merchant partners, and compliance officers.<\/p>\n\n\n\nA regulated payment institution is not automatically a low-risk payment institution. In the case of Fibonatix, the opposite question must be asked: can a historically high-risk processor prove that its new regulated structure has moved beyond the practices and networks of the past?<\/p>\n\n\n\nCall for Information<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram invites former employees, merchants, payment partners, victims, compliance officers, banks, acquirers, and insiders with knowledge of Fibonatix, its merchant portfolio, historic payment flows, or current Latvian acquiring setup to share information confidentially via Whistle42.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWe are particularly interested in information about merchant onboarding, high-risk vertical exposure, processing partners, chargeback patterns, related entities, and cross-border acquiring activity.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram will continue to monitor Fibonatix and the broader payment-processing infrastructure serving high-risk merchants in Europe.<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\n\nShare Information via Whistle42<\/a><\/div>\n<\/div>\n","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"Latvijas Banka has issued a payment institution licence to SIA Fibonatix (LV).
