report-threat-message\/","title":{"rendered":"Cyber Intimidation: FinTelegram Receives Threat Message Demanding Removal Of The Kingdom Bank Coverage"},"content":{"rendered":"\nEditorial note:<\/strong> Following publication, a representative of The Kingdom Bank contacted FinTelegram and denied that The Kingdom Bank, its management, employees, or team were involved in the email-bombing, DDoS pressure, or the Whistle42 threat message received by FinTelegram. The representative suggested that the message may have originated from a competitor, former employee, or unrelated third party. FinTelegram notes this denial.<\/em><\/p>\n\n\n\nShortly after FinTelegram published its Rail Atlas report<\/a> on The Kingdom Bank<\/strong>, Financial House<\/strong>, Jeton \/ La Orange<\/strong>, Speedy<\/strong>, Banky<\/strong>, and related payment-rail actors, FinTelegram experienced a wave of disruptive activity, including email-bombing<\/strong> and DDoS-style pressure<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\n<\/figure>\n<\/div>\n\n\nOn top of this, FinTelegram received a message through Whistle42<\/strong> from a sender referencing \u201cTheKingdomBank\u201d<\/strong>. The message demanded that FinTelegram remove all articles about The Kingdom Bank and threatened continued \u201cstress testing,\u201d disruption of FinTelegram\u2019s email systems, and legal action.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has preserved the message and related technical evidence. At this stage, FinTelegram has not independently verified<\/strong> whether the sender is authorised by, employed by, or formally connected to The Kingdom Bank. However, the message appears to link the ongoing disruption campaign with a demand to remove FinTelegram\u2019s reporting.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram will not remove evidence-based reporting in response to anonymous threats, cyber pressure, email-bombing, or intimidation attempts.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe Threat Message<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe message submitted through Whistle42 stated, in substance:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\u201cIf you don\u2019t remove all your articles about TheKingdomBank we will keep stress testing you, killing your emails and we will start moving legally against you.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/blockquote>\n\n\n\nThe sender identified the relevant brand or entity as \u201cTheKingdomBank\u201d<\/strong> and referenced FinTelegram\u2019s published Rail Atlas report<\/a><\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThis is not a normal correction request. It is not a formal right-of-reply communication. It is not a structured legal notice. It is a threat-style message that appears to demand removal of journalistic reporting under pressure.<\/p>\n\n\n\nRead our The Kingdom Bank” report here.<\/a><\/strong> <\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram\u2019s Position<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram welcomes legitimate corrections, legal notices, right-of-reply submissions, and factual clarifications. We routinely review such submissions and correct material where appropriate.<\/p>\n\n\n\nHowever, FinTelegram does not accept threats, cyber intimidation, email-bombing, DDoS-style disruption, or anonymous pressure campaigns as a basis for removing evidence-based reporting.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIf The Kingdom Bank, Financial House, Jeton, La Orange, Speedy, Banky, Plato \/ GoodFintech, or any related party believes that FinTelegram\u2019s reporting contains factual inaccuracies, they are invited to submit a detailed, attributable, evidence-based response.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram will review any such response under its editorial standards.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhy This Matters<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe timing of the message is significant. FinTelegram\u2019s Rail Atlas report mapped a payment and offshore-banking network involving:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nThe Kingdom Bank<\/strong> (www.kingdombank.com<\/a><\/em>), a Dominica offshore \/ international banking platform;<\/li>\n\n\n\nFinancial House Limited<\/strong> (www.financialhouse.io<\/a><\/em>), a UK FCA-authorised Electronic Money Institution currently subject to severe FCA supervisory restrictions;<\/li>\n\n\n\nSpeedy<\/strong> (www.speedy.io<\/a><\/em>), a Financial House trading name and Speedy-branded payment rail;<\/li>\n\n\n\nLa Orange \/ Jeton<\/strong>, an e-money services and wallet\/payment ecosystem;<\/li>\n\n\n\nJeton Bank<\/strong>, another Dominica offshore-banking node in the Jeton environment;<\/li>\n\n\n\nBanky<\/strong>, observed in The Kingdom Bank\u2019s instant-bank-transfer flow;<\/li>\n\n\n\nPlato \/ GoodFintech \/ KYXPlatform<\/strong>, observed in The Kingdom Bank\u2019s KYC and biometric-verification flow.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nThe report raised questions about offshore-to-EU payment rails, EU-facing onboarding, KYC outsourcing, Speedy-linked deposit infrastructure, Financial House\u2019s FCA restrictions, and the wider network-risk profile.<\/p>\n\n\n\nInstead of a formal factual response, FinTelegram received a threat-style message demanding deletion.<\/p>\n\n\n\nNo Attribution Without Evidence<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram is careful with attribution. We do not currently state<\/strong> that The Kingdom Bank itself, its management, employees, contractors, agents, affiliates, or service providers were behind the email-bombing or DDoS-style disruption or this message.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhat we can say is this:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nFinTelegram experienced disruptive cyber pressure after publication of the Rail Atlas report.<\/li>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram then received a Whistle42 message referencing \u201cTheKingdomBank.\u201d<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe message demanded removal of all articles about The Kingdom Bank.<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe message threatened continued \u201cstress testing\u201d and disruption of FinTelegram\u2019s emails.<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe message also threatened legal action.<\/li>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has preserved the submission and related evidence.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nThis is sufficient to classify the incident as a cyber-intimidation event<\/strong> connected to FinTelegram\u2019s The Kingdom Bank reporting.<\/p>\n\n\n\nEvidence Preservation<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has preserved the Whistle42 submission, screenshots, timestamps, and relevant technical logs. We are also preserving related email-bombing, server, firewall, and traffic evidence.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhere appropriate, FinTelegram may provide evidence to hosting providers, email infrastructure providers, cybersecurity specialists, regulators, law-enforcement agencies, and legal advisers.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram will not publish technical indicators that could compromise ongoing mitigation, source protection, or forensic preservation.<\/p>\n\n\n\nMessage To The Network<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram\u2019s position is simple:<\/p>\n\n\n\nCyber pressure will not remove evidence-based reporting.<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nThreats and disruption attempts will be documented, preserved, and, where appropriate, reported publicly. If any person or entity wishes to challenge FinTelegram\u2019s reporting, the proper path is clear: send a factual, attributable, evidence-based response. FinTelegram will review it.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhat will not work is anonymous intimidation.<\/p>\n\n\n\nCall For Information<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram invites whistleblowers, customers, former employees, compliance officers, payment insiders, cybersecurity specialists, hosting providers, and affected parties to provide information about:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nThe Kingdom Bank;<\/li>\n\n\n\nFinancial House Limited;<\/li>\n\n\n\nLa Orange \/ Jeton;<\/li>\n\n\n\nSpeedy and Speedy AG;<\/li>\n\n\n\nBanky \/ JSC CH GmbH;<\/li>\n\n\n\nJeton Bank;<\/li>\n\n\n\nPlato \/ GoodFintech \/ KYXPlatform;<\/li>\n\n\n\ncyber pressure, email-bombing, DDoS attempts, or intimidation campaigns connected to financial reporting;<\/li>\n\n\n\npayment flows involving offshore banks, EU payment rails, crypto, gambling, iGaming, forex, or high-risk merchants.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nWhistleblowers may contact FinTelegram via Whistle42. FinTelegram protects sources and distinguishes between documented facts, source-provided material, open-source intelligence, technical evidence, and editorial assessment.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nShare Information via Whistle42<\/a><\/div>\n<\/div>\n","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"Editorial note: Following publication, a representative of The Kingdom Bank contacted FinTelegram and denied that The Kingdom Bank, its management, employees, or team were involved in the email-bombing, DDoS pressure, or the Whistle42 threat message received by FinTelegram. FinTelegram continues its reporting and will not be intimidated.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nCyberattack Notice: FinTelegram Targeted by Email Bombing and DDoS Attacks After Softon Reports<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram informs its readers, whistleblowers, sources and partners that the platform has been facing sustained cyberattacks following our recent reporting on SOFTON LTD<\/strong>, Betzter<\/strong>, KingdomCasino<\/strong>, and related offshore casino and payment infrastructure.<\/p>\n\n\n\nRead our Softon reports here. <\/a><\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nOver the past days, FinTelegram has been confronted with a combination of email bombing<\/strong> and significant DDoS attacks<\/strong>. These attacks have, at times, made the FinTelegram website temporarily difficult or impossible to access for some readers.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWe have activated Cloudflare\u2019s \u201cUnder Attack\u201d<\/strong> mode and additional protective measures. While these measures help, the attacks remain disruptive.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram does not attribute responsibility for these attacks to any specific person, company or network at this stage. However, the timing is notable: the attacks started after FinTelegram intensified its reporting on the Softon casino cluster<\/strong>, including questions around Cyprus corporate structures, Anjouan licence claims, hidden cashier and PSP routes, player telemetry systems, open infrastructure leads, and EU consumer exposure.<\/p>\n\n\n\nDDoS attacks and email bombing are not arguments. They are digital pressure tactics. Their purpose is usually simple: overload systems, disrupt access, intimidate journalists, and make public-interest reporting more difficult.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThey will not stop FinTelegram.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIn the coming days, FinTelegram will publish a comprehensive Softon Compliance Report<\/strong>, mapping the broader infrastructure around SOFTON LTD, related casino brands, telemetry systems, technical leads, unresolved payment routes, and regulatory questions.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWe ask our readers for patience if access to FinTelegram is temporarily disrupted. We are working to keep the platform online, preserve evidence, protect sources, and continue publication.<\/p>\n\n\n\nTo whistleblowers and insiders: your information remains essential. If you have documents, payment records, technical evidence, KYC\/KYB files, corporate records, internal correspondence, casino cashier data, telemetry information, or knowledge about Softon, Betzter, KingdomCasino or related brands, please contact us securely via Whistle42<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram will continue to follow the rails \u2014 including the data rails, payment rails, corporate rails, and now the attack rails.<\/p>\n\n\n\nInvestigative journalism is not a crime. Cyber intimidation will not stop public-interest reporting.<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\n\nShare Information via Whistle42<\/a><\/div>\n<\/div>\n","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"Following FinTelegram\u2019s recent reporting on SOFTON LTD, Betzter, KingdomCasino and related offshore casino infrastructure, the FinTelegram platform has been hit by sustained email bombing and significant DDoS attacks. Casino That Watches Back: Betzter\u2019s Reported FullStory, Sentry Replay and Pusher Stack Raises EU Privacy Alarms"},"content":{"rendered":"\nSofton Ltd\u2019s offshore casino is not only a licensing problem. Technical evidence reviewed by FinTelegram suggests that Betzter may also operate a player-behaviour intelligence stack capable of linking identity, session replay, account events and sportsbook infrastructure outside the EU\u2019s licensed gambling perimeter. Betzter is not only a gambling-licence story. It is a data-protection story, a player-protection story, a behavioural-surveillance story, and a payment-transparency story.<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nBetzter.com, publicly operated by Cyprus-registered SOFTON LTD (HE 463977), presents itself as an Anjouan-licensed online casino. But the compliance problem may go far beyond gambling licensing. According to technical evidence reviewed by FinTelegram, Betzter\u2019s public application layer exposes a reported telemetry stack involving FullStory, Sentry Replay and Pusher \/ Laravel Echo private player-account channels. If confirmed, the configuration could enable identity-linked behavioural session recording, realtime balance and account-event tracking, and player-level profiling in an offshore casino environment outside the EU\u2019s licensed gambling and self-exclusion perimeter.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nKey Findings<\/h2>\n\n\n\n\nOperator anchor confirmed.<\/strong> Betzter.com publicly identifies SOFTON LTD<\/strong>, Cyprus registration number HE 463977<\/strong>, Nicosia, Cyprus, as owner\/operator. This is the cleanest legal-operator anchor currently available for Betzter.<\/li>\n\n\n\nAnjouan licence claim, not EU market access.<\/strong> Betzter publicly displays an Anjouan licence\/seal surface referencing ALSI-202409012-FI1<\/strong> and a unique seal identifier. FinTelegram treats this as a confirmed public licence claim, not as confirmation of licence validity, scope, covered domains, or authorisation to serve regulated EU markets.<\/li>\n\n\n\nReported dual session-replay stack.<\/strong> Technical evidence reviewed by FinTelegram reports the presence of FullStory<\/strong> and Sentry Replay<\/strong> in the Betzter application layer. This finding should be treated as a technical-source lead pending independent reproduction and preservation.<\/li>\n\n\n\nReported Sentry masking concern.<\/strong> According to the technical source, Sentry Replay was configured with maskAllInputs:false<\/strong>, maskAllText:false<\/strong> and blockAllMedia:false<\/strong>. If confirmed, this would represent a high-risk configuration for an online casino environment, particularly where player identity or account context is present.<\/li>\n\n\n\nIdentity linkage risk.<\/strong> The reported FullStory integration checks or maps player.uuid<\/strong> and player.email<\/strong>, potentially converting behavioural telemetry into an account-linked player dossier rather than anonymous analytics.<\/li>\n\n\n\nRealtime account-event stream.<\/strong> The Network Engineer Report identifies Pusher \/ Laravel Echo<\/strong> private player-account channel architecture, including playerAccount-style channels and account\/betting event logic. This suggests that Betzter is not a static casino facade but a realtime account and player-event application.<\/li>\n\n\n\nSportsbook \/ infrastructure bridge.<\/strong> Sentry configuration references sptpub.com<\/strong> and ui.invisiblesport.com<\/strong>, creating a sportsbook\/infrastructure bridge lead. This is not ownership proof, but it expands the data-perimeter and disclosure-target question.<\/li>\n\n\n\nGDPR and player-protection risk.<\/strong> The central issue is not merely that Betzter appears to operate outside EU gambling licensing regimes. The further issue is whether EU players are being observed, replayed, profiled and account-linked by an offshore operator outside the official CRUKS\/OASIS self-exclusion architecture.<\/li>\n\n\n\nPayment and AML black box remains.<\/strong> The public\/static evidence identifies visible payment-method branding but does not identify the cashier provider, PSP, merchant ID, gateway ID, connector ID, route UUID, acquirer or settlement bank. This makes the telemetry stack even more sensitive: player behaviour may be visible, while the supervised payment and KYC\/CDD chain remains opaque.<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nExecutive Summary<\/h2>\n\n\n\n<\/a><\/figure>\n<\/div>\n\n\nFinTelegram\u2019s is currently investigating the casino operator SOFTON LTD: a Cyprus company, an Anjouan licence narrative, EU-facing casino access, and unresolved payment infrastructure. This first report addresses a second<\/strong>, under-reported layer: player surveillance infrastructure<\/strong>. According to technical evidence reviewed by FinTelegram, Betzter\u2019s public application layer reportedly includes a combination of:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nFullStory<\/strong> behavioural session analytics;<\/li>\n\n\n\nSentry Replay<\/strong> error\/session replay telemetry;<\/li>\n\n\n\nPusher \/ Laravel Echo<\/strong> realtime private player-account channels;<\/li>\n\n\n\nplayer UUID and email-context logic;<\/li>\n\n\n\nsportsbook\/infrastructure markers referencing SPTPub<\/strong> and InvisibleSport<\/strong>.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nThis architecture would allow reconstruction of what a player did, saw, clicked, typed, attempted, failed, deposited, lost, won or triggered at account-event level \u2014 while that player may be interacting with an offshore casino outside the EU\u2019s licensed gambling perimeter. This is the core FinTelegram finding:<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe illegal-casino problem is only the first layer. The second layer is the behavioural-surveillance problem.<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nAn offshore operator that is not licensed in the Netherlands, Germany, Austria or other regulated EU markets may nevertheless maintain persistent player identities, session histories, behavioural patterns, balance updates and betting-event data. In other words, the operator may know the player in extraordinary detail \u2014 without being integrated into the official player-protection systems designed to protect that player.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram does not allege that SOFTON LTD has committed a criminal offence or that the telemetry was misused. The issue is the risk architecture: a high-risk gambling environment, reported identity-linked session replay, reported masking-disabled replay configuration, private realtime account channels, and an unresolved payment\/KYC chain.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThat combination should concern data-protection authorities, gambling regulators, payment supervisors and consumer-protection agencies.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nWhy Session Replay Matters In Online Gambling<\/h2>\n\n\n\nSession replay is not ordinary page analytics. Properly configured, it may help a website operator debug errors, improve user experience and detect friction. Poorly governed, it can reconstruct a user\u2019s digital behaviour at individual-session level: clicks, scrolls, navigation paths, visible text, form interactions, error states and interface context. In ordinary e-commerce, that is already sensitive. In online gambling, it becomes far more serious. A casino session is not a normal shopping journey. It may reveal:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nimpulsive betting behaviour;<\/li>\n\n\n\nfailed deposits;<\/li>\n\n\n\nrepeated failed bets;<\/li>\n\n\n\nbonus-seeking or loss-chasing patterns;<\/li>\n\n\n\naccount balance changes;<\/li>\n\n\n\nbetting limits;<\/li>\n\n\n\nsession duration and late-night play;<\/li>\n\n\n\npayment attempts;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwithdrawal friction;<\/li>\n\n\n\npossible indicators of gambling disorder;<\/li>\n\n\n\nidentity-linked emotional or behavioural vulnerability.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nIn a licensed environment, gambling regulators impose controls around player protection, responsible gambling, self-exclusion, affordability, AML and complaint handling. In an offshore casino environment, those protections may be absent or structurally weakened.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThat is why the reported Betzter stack matters.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nThe Reported Player-Telemetry Stack<\/h2>\n\n\n\nData Layer<\/th>Reported Tool \/ Mechanism<\/th>Why It Matters<\/th>Evidentiary Status<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>Session behaviour<\/td>FullStory<\/a><\/td>Potentially reconstructs clicks, scrolls, navigation and user interaction sequence<\/td>Technical-source lead<\/td><\/tr>Error\/session replay<\/td>Sentry Replay<\/a><\/td>Reconstructs user sessions around errors and events; privacy depends heavily on masking configuration<\/td>Technical-source lead<\/td><\/tr>Identity mapping<\/td>player.uuid \/ player.email<\/td>May convert behavioural telemetry into account-linked player data<\/td>Technical-source lead<\/td><\/tr>Realtime account events<\/td>Pusher<\/a> \/ Laravel Echo<\/a> private channels<\/td>Supports balance updates, session events, failed-bet events and account-state signals<\/td>Primary technical evidence<\/td><\/tr>Sportsbook bridge<\/td>sptpub.com \/ ui.invisiblesport.com<\/td>Extends the infrastructure\/data perimeter beyond Betzter\u2019s own domain<\/td>Primary technical lead<\/td><\/tr>Payment surface<\/td>Card\/crypto\/alternative payment branding<\/td>Public payment methods visible but cashier\/PSP route remains undisclosed<\/td>Primary gap<\/td><\/tr>Cashier\/PSP route<\/td>Unknown<\/td>Merchant ID, gateway ID, connector ID, acquirer and settlement path not visible<\/td>Missing nuclear evidence<\/td><\/tr><\/tbody><\/table><\/figure>\n\n\n\nThe critical point is the combination. A single analytics tool can be lawful and legitimate. Realtime account events can be normal. Error monitoring can be necessary. But when these tools operate together in an offshore casino environment, with reported identity mapping and unclear masking, the compliance risk changes. The system becomes capable of creating an identity-linked behavioural dossier on each player.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nGDPR Risk Assessment<\/h2>\n\n\n\nAssessed against Regulation (EU) 2016\/679 as initial regulatory risk, not a legal opinion. Two parallel session-replay systems are hard to reconcile with data minimisation (Art. 5(1)(c))<\/strong>; a single, properly masked system would satisfy any UX or debugging purpose. The reported masking-disabled Sentry configuration runs against the expectation \u2014 expressed by the CNIL and other EU authorities \u2014 that individual-level session replay is high-risk processing requiring comprehensive masking and, ordinarily, prior consent (Art. 6). The reported absence of any consent or disclosure layer on the public surface is a material transparency concern (Arts. 13\u201314), and disabling masking by default inverts data-protection-by-default (Art. 25).<\/p>\n\n\n\nRouting email\/UUID to US processors is not unlawful per se. The EU\u2013US Data Privacy Framework remains a valid adequacy decision \u2014 it survived the Latombe challenge<\/a><\/strong> before the General Court in September 2025 and is under appeal to the CJEU (Case C-703\/25 P). Transfers are lawful where the recipient is DPF-certified or covered by SCCs plus a transfer impact assessment. The genuine risk is the absence of any disclosed transfer mechanism<\/strong> and FullStory\u2019s public documentation still citing the defunct Privacy Shield rather than current DPF certification. Large-scale, systematic, identity-linked behavioural monitoring in a high-risk sector squarely triggers the Art. 35 DPIA obligation.<\/p>\n\n\n\nHere are our questions in this context:<\/p>\n\n\n\n1. Lawful Basis<\/h3>\n\n\n\nThe first question is simple: on what lawful basis does SOFTON LTD process session-replay and player-level behavioural telemetry?<\/p>\n\n\n\nFor a normal analytics cookie, consent may be required depending on local implementation and ePrivacy rules. For identity-linked session replay in a gambling context<\/strong>, legitimate interest becomes much harder to sustain without a detailed balancing test, clear disclosure, strict minimisation and robust masking.<\/p>\n\n\n\nA high-risk offshore casino cannot treat behavioural session reconstruction as invisible background plumbing.<\/p>\n\n\n\n2. Transparency<\/h3>\n\n\n\nPlayers must be told what is being collected, by whom, for what purpose, for how long, and with whom the data is shared.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIf Betzter\u2019s public surface does not clearly disclose the use of session replay, behavioural analytics, realtime account-event monitoring and processors such as FullStory, Sentry or Pusher, the transparency risk is serious<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe fact that a tool is common in software development does not remove the controller\u2019s GDPR transparency obligations.<\/p>\n\n\n\n3. Data Minimisation<\/h3>\n\n\n\nThe reported dual-replay setup raises a basic minimisation question:<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhy would a casino need both FullStory and Sentry Replay running in parallel on player sessions?<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nA single properly masked tool may be sufficient for debugging or user-experience analysis. Two overlapping systems may create redundancy, but redundancy is not the same as necessity.<\/p>\n\n\n\nUnder GDPR, the operator must be able to explain why the data is adequate, relevant and limited to what is necessary.<\/p>\n\n\n\n4. Masking and Data Protection by Default<\/h3>\n\n\n\nAccording to the technical source, Sentry Replay was configured with:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nmaskAllInputs:false;<\/li>\n\n\n\nmaskAllText:false;<\/li>\n\n\n\nblockAllMedia:false.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nIf confirmed, this would be a major red flag.<\/p>\n\n\n\nSentry\u2019s own privacy-oriented baseline masks text and blocks media by default. Turning masking and blocking off, especially in an online casino interface, materially increases the risk that player-visible text, account context, balance information, interface states or other sensitive data could be reconstructed.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThis does not prove that passwords, payment credentials or documents were captured. It does mean that the operator and processor must explain the configuration, masking rules, field exclusions and actual data captured.<\/p>\n\n\n\n5. DPIA Requirement<\/h3>\n\n\n\nLarge-scale, systematic, identity-linked behavioural monitoring in an online gambling environment should trigger serious consideration of a GDPR Article 35 Data Protection Impact Assessment.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe DPIA question is unavoidable:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nWas a DPIA conducted?<\/li>\n\n\n\nDid it assess session replay?<\/li>\n\n\n\nDid it assess identity mapping?<\/li>\n\n\n\nDid it assess gambling vulnerability?<\/li>\n\n\n\nDid it assess EU player exposure?<\/li>\n\n\n\nDid it assess international transfers?<\/li>\n\n\n\nDid it assess processor access?<\/li>\n\n\n\nDid it assess retention and deletion?<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nIf no DPIA exists, that would be a significant governance failure.<\/p>\n\n\n\n6. International Transfers<\/h3>\n\n\n\nThe transfer issue should be framed accurately.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe use of U.S.-linked processors such as FullStory or Sentry is not automatically unlawful. The EU-U.S. Data Privacy Framework remains in force following the General Court\u2019s September 2025 Latombe judgment, although an appeal is pending before the Court of Justice of the European Union. Transfers may be lawful where the recipient is DPF-certified or where Standard Contractual Clauses and a transfer impact assessment are properly implemented.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe relevant question is not whether every U.S.-linked processor transfer is per se unlawful. The relevant question is whether SOFTON LTD disclosed the transfer mechanism, identified the processors, documented the legal basis, and implemented adequate masking and minimisation controls for high-risk gambling telemetry.<\/p>\n\n\n\nGDPR Risk Rating<\/h3>\n\n\n\nOverall GDPR Risk: HIGH<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nEscalation to CRITICAL if independently confirmed:<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\n\nSentry Replay masking disabled;<\/li>\n\n\n\nFullStory identity mapping to player.email and player.uuid;<\/li>\n\n\n\nabsence of player-facing disclosure or consent;<\/li>\n\n\n\nabsence of DPIA;<\/li>\n\n\n\nbroad processor access to EU-origin gambling behavioural data.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nSelf-Exclusion and Player-Protection Risk<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe concern is not that the telemetry stack proves active circumvention of CRUKS or OASIS. The concern is more structural. CRUKS in the Netherlands<\/strong> and OASIS in Germany<\/strong> are official self-exclusion frameworks within licensed gambling markets. Offshore operators outside those licensed perimeters are typically not integrated into these systems.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIf Betzter reaches EU players without local licences<\/strong>, it sits outside the very protection architecture designed to prevent self-excluded or vulnerable individuals from gambling. At the same time, according to the technical evidence reviewed by FinTelegram, the operator may maintain its own persistent player identity, session history and account-event stream. This creates the paradox:<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe regulator may not see the player \u2014 but the offshore casino may see everything.<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nA player excluded from a licensed market could still be tracked by an offshore operator through email, UUID, session replay, account events and behaviour patterns. That does not prove exploitation. It does show why unlicensed casino telemetry is a consumer-protection problem, not merely a privacy topic.<\/p>\n\n\n\nPlayer-Protection Risk Rating<\/h3>\n\n\n\nHIGH<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nThe risk is highest where the same system can identify player behaviour, account state, betting failures, balance changes, and session patterns without being subject to licensed-market responsible-gambling controls.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nAML and Payment-Transparency Risk<\/h2>\n\n\n\n<\/a><\/figure>\n<\/div>\n\n\nThe AML concern is not that session replay is a laundering tool. The concern is that the same environment which appears capable of maintaining identity-linked behavioural and account-event data does not disclose, at public level, the supervised payment, KYC\/CDD, merchant-of-record and settlement chain. The Network Engineer Report <\/strong>identifies a payment black box:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\npayment methods are visible;<\/li>\n\n\n\ncashier provider not identified;<\/li>\n\n\n\nPSP not identified;<\/li>\n\n\n\nMID not identified;<\/li>\n\n\n\ngateway ID not identified;<\/li>\n\n\n\nconnector ID not identified;<\/li>\n\n\n\nroute UUID not identified;<\/li>\n\n\n\nacquirer not identified;<\/li>\n\n\n\nApple Pay merchant ID not identified.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nIn an offshore gambling environment, this opacity prevents external assessment of whether player identity, deposits, withdrawals, AML risk signals, chargebacks, refunds and complaints are subject to proper regulated monitoring.<\/p>\n\n\n\nA licensed operator should be able to explain who processes payments, who holds merchant accounts, what merchant category code is used, who performs KYC\/CDD, and which regulator supervises the payment chain.<\/p>\n\n\n\nAML \/ Payment Risk Rating<\/h3>\n\n\n\nMEDIUM\u2013HIGH<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nThe high component is structural: offshore gambling, visible payment surface, hidden payment route, and identity-linked account architecture. The telemetry-specific AML inference remains medium-confidence until payment and KYC flows are obtained.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nThe SPTPub \/ InvisibleSport Perimeter Question<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe Network Engineer Report identifies references to sptpub.com<\/strong> and ui.invisiblesport.com<\/strong> in the Betzter technical layer. It also identifies api.sptpub.com<\/strong> resolving to a direct Hetzner infrastructure lead in Germany.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThis matters for privacy and compliance because it expands the perimeter.<\/p>\n\n\n\nA player may believe they are interacting with Betzter. In technical terms, the application may involve multiple infrastructure and telemetry parties. Without processor disclosure, contractual transparency and data-flow mapping, neither players nor regulators can assess:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nwho receives data;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwho controls the processing;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhich processors are involved;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhere data is hosted;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhat retention applies;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether data is used across brands;<\/li>\n\n\n\nwhether sportsbook, casino, CRM and payment systems share identifiers.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram does not claim that SPTPub or InvisibleSport owns Betzter. They are disclosure targets.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nRegulatory Referral Recommendations<\/h2>\n\n\n\nDutch Autoriteit Persoonsgegevens<\/h3>\n\n\n\nThe AP should assess whether Dutch data subjects are exposed to session replay, identity-linked behavioural telemetry or private account-event monitoring by an offshore casino operator outside the licensed Dutch gambling perimeter.<\/p>\n\n\n\nCypriot Commissioner for Personal Data Protection<\/h3>\n\n\n\nSOFTON LTD is a Cyprus company. The Cypriot data protection authority should assess controller status, processor disclosures, DPIA, session-replay safeguards, transfer mechanisms, and whether the one-stop-shop framework is applicable.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIrish Data Protection Commission<\/h3>\n\n\n\nIf FullStory, Sentry or other processors rely on an Irish establishment for EU processing operations, the DPC may be relevant for processor-side compliance, transfer documentation and cross-border coordination.<\/p>\n\n\n\nDutch Kansspelautoriteit<\/h3>\n\n\n\nThe KSA should assess whether Betzter reaches Dutch consumers without a Dutch licence and whether its telemetry architecture creates additional risks for self-excluded or vulnerable players outside CRUKS.<\/p>\n\n\n\nGerman GGL<\/h3>\n\n\n\nThe GGL should assess the German self-exclusion and player-protection dimension, including whether German players can access Betzter or related brands outside the OASIS framework.<\/p>\n\n\n\nEuropean Data Protection Board<\/h3>\n\n\n\nThe EDPB should consider the broader policy issue: session replay and identity-linked behavioural monitoring in unlicensed or offshore gambling environments that serve EU data subjects.<\/p>\n\n\n\nEuropol EC3<\/h3>\n\n\n\nEC3 should be notified of the broader cyberfinancial structure: offshore casino, Cyprus operator, direct infrastructure lead, telemetry stack, undisclosed payment route, and potential cross-brand operational pattern.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nQuestions for SOFTON LTD \/ Betzter<\/h2>\n\n\n\n\nDoes SOFTON LTD confirm that it operates Casino Exposed: Gadzooks Limited, A Cura\u00e7ao Share Shuffle, And The Larnaca Fiduciary Hub Behind The Third Pillar Of The Cyprus Casino Cluster!"},"content":{"rendered":"\nHow a Cura\u00e7ao-linked casino environment, a Cyprus-facing payment vehicle, recurring fiduciary-service providers, and open-banking payment leads raise urgent questions for EU gambling, AML, and PSD2 supervisors.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n2-Minutes Briefing<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram has reviewed Cyprus registry documents, whistleblower submissions, open-source casino disclosures, and correspondence concerning Trueluck Casino<\/a><\/strong>, Gadzooks Limited<\/a><\/strong>, and a recurring fiduciary-service structure in Larnaca, Cyprus.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe available records identify Gadzooks Limited<\/strong> \u2014 a Cyprus private company registered under HE 438503<\/strong> \u2014 as a Cyprus-facing vehicle within the Luckywayz<\/strong> casino environment. Registry filings reviewed by FinTelegram show that Gadzooks was previously wholly owned by Luckywayz Limited B.V.<\/a><\/strong>, a Cura\u00e7ao entity, before a minority shareholding was transferred to Richard John Green<\/a><\/strong>, a UK national resident in Malta, on 31 May 2024.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe documents further show that Georgia Nikoletti<\/a><\/strong>, formally described in Cyprus registry filings as \u03a5\u03a0\u0391\u039b\u039b\u0397\u039b\u039f\u03a3<\/strong> \u2014 employee \u2014 was appointed sole director of Gadzooks Limited on 10 October 2023. On the same date, P.Z.T. Services Ltd<\/a><\/strong> became corporate secretary, while the previous director and secretary were removed. This coordinated fiduciary handover mirrors patterns identified by FinTelegram in other casino-linked Cyprus structures.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe significance of this third instalment is not that one more offshore casino brand has surfaced. It is that the available registry pattern points to a recurring Larnaca-based fiduciary hub servicing multiple casino-linked Cyprus vehicles connected to offshore ownership structures, EU-facing payment flows, and open-banking payment rails.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhistleblower material further alleges a three-layer payment architecture involving PAYTECH LTD<\/a><\/strong> as a possible routing\/orchestration layer, Yapily<\/a> \/ Yapily Connect UAB<\/a><\/strong> as an open-banking front-end, and ISX Financial EU PLC<\/a><\/strong> as a potential banking or EMI endpoint. FinTelegram has not independently verified the alleged PAYTECH or ISX links and treats them as investigative leads requiring confirmation through payment records, KYB files, settlement reports, or right-of-reply responses. The Yapily element is supported by correspondence reviewed by FinTelegram, but the precise merchant, platform, or partner relationship through which the transactions allegedly entered Yapily\u2019s rails remains to be established.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram makes no allegation of personal wrongdoing against registered directors, employees, lawyers, corporate-service providers, or named individuals. The issue is structural: whether Cyprus corporate-service infrastructure, Cura\u00e7ao ownership, nominee-style directorships, and EU payment rails are being combined in a way that enables offshore casino operators to reach restricted EU markets while obscuring beneficial ownership, merchant responsibility, and consumer remedies.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nKey Data Table: Trueluck \/ Gadzooks \/ Larnaca Fiduciary Hub<\/h2>\n\n\n\nCategory<\/th>Name \/ Identifier<\/th>Jurisdiction<\/th>Role in the case<\/th>Evidence status<\/th>Compliance relevance<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>Casino brand<\/td>Trueluck<\/a> \/ Trueluck Casino<\/a><\/td>Online \/ offshore<\/td>Casino brand referenced in whistleblower material and public casino disclosures<\/td>Corroborated as a brand; Services Ltd, Potens Corporate Services Ltd, and related filings;<\/li>\n\n\n\nForm HE4 and HE57 filings concerning director\/secretary changes and share transfers;<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhistleblower submissions concerning Trueluck, payment flows, and customer complaints;<\/li>\n\n\n\nCorrespondence reviewed by FinTelegram concerning Yapily\u2019s handling of an affected player complaint;<\/li>\n\n\n\nPublic casino-facing and open-source material concerning Trueluck, Luckywayz, Gadzooks, and related brand disclosures;<\/li>\n\n\n\nPrior FinTelegram reporting on the first two pillars of the Cyprus casino payment-tech cluster.<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram distinguishes between:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nRegistry-established facts<\/strong> \u2014 based on official corporate filings reviewed by FinTelegram;<\/li>\n\n\n\nCasino-facing evidence<\/strong> \u2014 based on website disclosures, casino terms, public brand pages, or payment-agent statements;<\/li>\n\n\n\nCorrespondence on file<\/strong> \u2014 based on communications reviewed by FinTelegram;<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhistleblower leads<\/strong> \u2014 credible but not yet independently verified allegations;<\/li>\n\n\n\nCompliance analysis<\/strong> \u2014 FinTelegram\u2019s interpretation of the risk implications.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nNo statement in this report should be read as an allegation of proven criminal conduct by any named person or entity.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nIntroduction: The Third Pillar Under Review<\/h2>\n\n\n\nOn 9 May 2026, FinTelegram reported<\/a> how Dutch forensic action exposed the HolyLuck \/ InstantCasino<\/strong> cluster \u2014 a set of casino brands operating through shared infrastructure and linked to Dutch consumer losses. On 27 May 2026, FinTelegram identified the second pillar: Mega.bet<\/strong>, connected through Belize parent Global Nexus Ltd<\/strong> and Cyprus vehicle IMMIX Solutions Ltd<\/strong>, with a recurring fiduciary-service pattern. This third instalment concerns Trueluck Casino<\/strong> and Gadzooks Limited<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nRead our HolyLuck reports here. <\/a><\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nThe documented overlaps now make coincidence increasingly difficult to maintain as the sole explanation. The available registry pattern points to a recurring Larnaca fiduciary hub<\/strong> in which Cyprus companies connected to offshore casino ownership structures are administered through overlapping secretarial, correspondence, and employee-director arrangements.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe central issue is not merely whether one casino brand is licensed or unlicensed. The issue is whether Cyprus-based corporate-service infrastructure is being used to create EU-facing companies that sit between offshore casino operators, European consumers, and regulated payment rails.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nTrueluck Casino and Gadzooks Limited<\/h2>\n\n\n\nPublicly available Trueluck operator attribution is not fully consistent across sources. Some sources and disclosures connect the environment to Luckywayz Limited B.V.<\/strong>, while others refer to different operator entities or jurisdictions. This inconsistency is itself a compliance red flag, suggesting possible brand fragmentation, affiliate mirrors, operator rotation, or uneven disclosure standards.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram therefore treats Gadzooks Limited as a Cyprus-facing vehicle linked to the Luckywayz \/ Trueluck casino environment<\/strong>, rather than asserting at this stage that Gadzooks is the definitive legal operator of Trueluck Casino in every context. That said, registry and payment-agent disclosures reviewed by FinTelegram establish a significant structural connection:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nGadzooks Limited is a Cyprus company registered under HE 438503<\/strong>;<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe company was previously wholly owned by Luckywayz Limited B.V.<\/strong> of Cura\u00e7ao;<\/li>\n\n\n\nOn 31 May 2024, 100 of 900 ordinary shares were transferred to Richard John Green<\/strong>, a UK national registered with an address in Birgu, Malta;<\/li>\n\n\n\nAfter the transfer, Luckywayz retained approximately 88.9% of Gadzooks Limited;<\/li>\n\n\n\nGadzooks appears in public casino-payment contexts as a Cyprus payment agent or payment-processing vehicle in the Luckywayz casino environment.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nThis structure creates an obvious question for banks, PSPs, EMIs, and open-banking providers: when onboarding or processing for Gadzooks Limited, did they treat it as an ordinary Cyprus company, or did they identify the underlying offshore casino exposure, Cura\u00e7ao ownership, and restricted-market gambling risk?<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nThe Share Shuffle: Cura\u00e7ao Ownership and a Malta-Based UK Minority Shareholder<\/h2>\n\n\n\nForm HE57, the share-transfer filing dated 31 May 2024 and reviewed by FinTelegram, records that Luckywayz Limited B.V.<\/strong> held all 900 ordinary shares of Gadzooks Limited. The filing documents the transfer of 100 ordinary shares<\/strong> \u2014 approximately 11.1%<\/strong> \u2014 to Richard John Green<\/strong>, a UK national described as a businessman with an address in Birgu, Malta.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe filing establishes a registry-confirmed structural link between a Cura\u00e7ao casino entity and an EU-facing Cyprus company. What remains open is the commercial rationale for the minority stake:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nWhat consideration was paid?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhy was the transfer made in May 2024?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWas the transfer connected to licensing, banking, payment onboarding, governance, or risk distribution?<\/li>\n\n\n\nDoes Mr Green exercise any operational, advisory, or beneficial role?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWas the transfer disclosed to PSPs, banks, open-banking providers, or casino counterparties?<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram invites Mr Green and Gadzooks Limited to provide clarification.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nThe Officer Structure: Coordinated Fiduciary Handover<\/h2>\n\n\n\nForm HE4 records that on 10 October 2023<\/strong>, Georgia Nikoletti<\/strong> was appointed sole director of Gadzooks Limited and P.Z.T. Services Ltd<\/strong> was appointed corporate secretary. The same filing records the simultaneous resignation of the previous secretary, Nexellence Fiduciary Services Ltd<\/strong>, and the previous director, Afroditi Kittou<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThis was not a random administrative update. It was a coordinated fiduciary handover: director and secretary changed on the same day, with the company moving into the P.Z.T. orbit<\/strong>. A secretary\u2019s certificate signed by P.Z.T. Services Ltd on 31 May 2024 confirms that the HE57 share transfer reflected the register maintained at the company\u2019s registered office, placing P.Z.T. Services Ltd at the administrative centre of the ownership change.<\/p>\n\n\n\nCorrespondence for the relevant filings was handled by Potens Corporate Services Ltd<\/strong>, Leoforos Archiepiskopou Makariou III 84, Shop\/Office 1, Larnaca<\/strong>, the address that recurs throughout FinTelegram\u2019s Cyprus casino payment-tech series.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nThe Nominee-Style Director Pattern<\/h2>\n\n\n\nAcross the three pillars investigated by FinTelegram, the registry record shows a recurring personnel and fiduciary-service pattern:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nGeorgia Nikoletti<\/strong> \u2014 registry occupation: \u03a5\u03a0\u0391\u039b\u039b\u0397\u039b\u039f\u03a3<\/strong>, i.e., employee \u2014 appears as sole director of Gadzooks Limited<\/strong> and, according to documents reviewed by FinTelegram, also directs IMMIX Solutions Ltd<\/strong>, the Cyprus vehicle linked to the Mega.bet pillar.<\/li>\n\n\n\nArtem Rak<\/strong> appears as director of S.I.L.V.E.R Veil Group Ltd<\/strong>, the processing vehicle connected to the InstantCasino \/ HolyLuck pillar.<\/li>\n\n\n\nSecretarial and correspondence functions repeatedly involve P.Z.T. Services Ltd<\/strong>, Potens Corporate Services Ltd<\/strong>, or related Larnaca corporate-service infrastructure.<\/li>\n\n\n\nOwnership sits with offshore Cura\u00e7ao or Belize structures.<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\nTo be clear: FinTelegram makes no allegation of personal wrongdoing against Ms Nikoletti, Mr Rak, or any individual employee.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe observation is structural. Where a sole director is formally described in registry filings as an employee, this raises the question whether such person can, in practice, exercise the independent oversight, mind-and-management, and fiduciary judgment that directorship under Cyprus company law presupposes.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe compliance consequences are serious. Beneficial-ownership registers are only as good as their inputs. When a registered director is an employee, the shareholder is an offshore company, and the actual commercial activity is high-risk gambling, KYB checks that stop at the face of the registry risk recording form rather than substance.<\/p>\n\n\n\nUnder the EU AML framework, obliged entities must identify the natural persons who ultimately own or control the customer. A structure that results in no operational controller appearing in any EU registry should trigger enhanced due diligence, not routine onboarding.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n<\/figure>\n<\/div>\n\n\nThe Larnaca Fiduciary Hub<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe documented facts are narrower than the broadest whistleblower allegations, but they are significant. P.Z.T. Services Ltd<\/strong>, Potens Corporate Services Ltd<\/strong>, and multiple casino-linked Cyprus vehicles are associated through recurring filings, secretarial roles, correspondence roles, and the address at Leoforos Archiepiskopou Makariou III 84, Larnaca<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe coordinated migrations of secretarial control into P.Z.T. Services Ltd \u2014 from other fiduciary-service providers in multiple casino-linked vehicles \u2014 point to deliberate, centralised restructuring rather than random administrative movement.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram therefore treats the Larnaca address and P.Z.T.\/Potens pattern as a fiduciary hub indicator<\/strong>.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIt is not yet proof of ultimate control, beneficial ownership, or operational command. Those questions require access to UBO filings, client files, banking records, internal service agreements, and regulatory responses.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nThe Toulouras Lead: Person of Interest, Not Established Controller<\/h2>\n\n\n\nWhistleblower submissions reviewed by FinTelegram identify Panayiotis \/ Panos Toulouras<\/strong>, a Cyprus attorney, as the alleged principal behind P.Z.T. Services Ltd and the wider Larnaca fiduciary hub. FinTelegram has not independently verified this allegation.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram does not currently hold a registry document naming Mr Toulouras as owner, director, or controller of P.Z.T. Services Ltd. Nothing in this report should be read as asserting that Mr Toulouras controls P.Z.T. Services Ltd, Gadzooks Limited, or the casino-payment network.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhat is documented is narrower: entities associated with the Larnaca hub and companies said by whistleblowers to be connected to Toulouras appear in the same geographic and fiduciary-service context. This creates a legitimate verification question that Cyprus authorities, with full registry, UBO, AML-supervision, and professional-conduct access, should be able to resolve.<\/p>\n\n\n\nMr Toulouras is invited to respond. FinTelegram will publish any substantive reply.<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nThe Payment Architecture: PAYTECH, Yapily, and ISX Financial<\/h2>\n\n\n\nWhistleblower material describes a three-layer payment system allegedly moving EU consumer funds into the Trueluck \/ Gadzooks environment. Each layer carries a different evidentiary weight.<\/p>\n\n\n\nLayer A \u2014 PAYTECH LTD \/ pay.tech<\/h3>\n\n\n\nStatus: whistleblower lead \u2014 unverified<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nPAYTECH is described by whistleblowers as a white-label routing and smart-orchestration layer capable of connecting high-risk merchants to multiple payment providers, gateways, and settlement endpoints. FinTelegram has not independently verified a contractual relationship between PAYTECH LTD and Gadzooks Limited, Trueluck, Luckywayz, or the wider casino cluster.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe key questions are:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nDid PAYTECH provide checkout, routing, orchestration, cascading, or merchant-technical services to any entity in the Trueluck \/ Luckywayz \/ Gadzooks environment?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhat is PAYTECH\u2019s regulatory status?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhich PSPs, EMIs, banks, PISPs, or acquirers did it connect to?<\/li>\n\n\n\nDid it process or route transactions for unlicensed-in-market casino operators?<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nPAYTECH is invited to clarify its role.<\/p>\n\n\n\nLayer B \u2014 Yapily \/ Yapily Connect UAB<\/h3>\n\n\n\nStatus: partially corroborated \u2014 correspondence on file<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nWhistleblower material and correspondence reviewed by FinTelegram indicate that open-banking rails associated with Yapily<\/strong> were involved in an affected player\u2019s payment flow.<\/p>\n\n\n\nYapily\u2019s own position, as reflected in correspondence reviewed by FinTelegram, is that it is an API infrastructure provider facilitating Account Information Services and Payment Initiation Services. Yapily states that it does not open bank accounts, hold funds, control customer funds, intervene in payments, or determine the account information relating to a particular transaction.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThat may accurately describe the payment-initiation plumbing. But it does not answer the core compliance question: through which merchant, platform, or partner relationship did the alleged Gadzooks \/ Trueluck payment flows enter Yapily\u2019s rails, and what merchant-use-case monitoring was applied?<\/p>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram does not assert at this stage that Yapily directly onboarded Trueluck, Gadzooks, or Luckywayz as a merchant. The relevant question is broader and more precise:<\/p>\n\n\n\nHow did a gambling-related payment flow connected to an offshore casino environment allegedly pass through regulated open-banking infrastructure, and what controls were applied at onboarding and during monitoring?<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nLayer C \u2014 ISX Financial EU PLC<\/h3>\n\n\n\nStatus: whistleblower lead \u2014 unverified<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nWhistleblower material alleges that ISX Financial EU PLC<\/strong>, an EU-licensed electronic money institution, may have acted as a banking or EMI endpoint in the payment architecture. FinTelegram has not independently verified this allegation.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe open questions are:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nDid ISX Financial maintain accounts, settlement relationships, merchant relationships, or technical integrations connected to Gadzooks Limited, Luckywayz, Trueluck, PAYTECH, or related casino brands?<\/li>\n\n\n\nIf so, what KYB was performed?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWas Luckywayz Limited B.V. identified as majority shareholder of Gadzooks?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhat merchant category coding and transaction descriptors were used?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWere gambling-risk, Cura\u00e7ao-ownership, and restricted-market indicators escalated?<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nISX Financial is invited to respond.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nYapily\u2019s Compliance Response: Denial, Closure, Acknowledgement, Silence<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe sequence described in correspondence reviewed by FinTelegram is concerning.<\/p>\n\n\n\nAccording to the correspondence, when first contacted by an affected player about transactions linked to the Trueluck \/ Gadzooks environment, Yapily\u2019s compliance function stated that it could not locate the transactions in its system and did not know who the payment operator was. In the same communication, the complaint was treated as closed, while the player was invited to provide further information and informed of escalation rights.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhen the player later provided bank-certified transaction metadata explicitly identifying Yapily in the data-exchange chain, the company acknowledged activity and said the matter had been escalated for internal review.<\/p>\n\n\n\nAccording to the whistleblower, no substantive follow-up has since been received.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIf accurately reflected by the correspondence reviewed by FinTelegram, the sequence is consistent with what consumers experience as a systemic compliance failure: initial denial, reversal under documentary pressure, internal escalation, and subsequent silence.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe issue is not whether Yapily held the funds. The issue is whether regulated open-banking infrastructure was used in a payment flow connected to an offshore casino environment, and whether Yapily\u2019s onboarding, partner monitoring, transaction visibility, complaint handling, and suspicious-activity procedures were adequate.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nDutch Market and CRUKS: The Consumer-Protection Gap<\/h2>\n\n\n\nPublicly available material and whistleblower submissions suggest that Trueluck appears to reach Dutch-speaking players. FinTelegram has also reviewed material suggesting that affected players in the Netherlands were able to deposit through payment methods presented in the casino environment.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe CRUKS point must be framed precisely.<\/p>\n\n\n\nCRUKS is the Dutch self-exclusion register within the Dutch licensed gambling perimeter. Offshore operators that do not hold a KSA licence are typically outside that protection architecture. That is precisely the problem.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIf Trueluck or related domains reached Dutch consumers without a KSA licence and without CRUKS integration, then Dutch self-excluded players may have been exposed to a casino environment outside the very safeguards designed to protect them.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThis is not merely a technical licensing issue. It is a consumer-protection failure created by cross-border regulatory arbitrage.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe relevant question for the Dutch Kansspelautoriteit is therefore not only whether Trueluck has a KSA licence. The question is whether the operator, affiliates, payment providers, and technical intermediaries enabled Dutch player acquisition, deposits, and losses despite the Dutch regulatory perimeter.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nEU Regulatory Dimension: A Licensing-Circumvention Architecture?<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe Cura\u00e7ao \u2192 Cyprus \u2192 EU structure has the characteristics of a licensing-circumvention architecture.<\/p>\n\n\n\nNational regimes such as the Dutch KSA \/ CRUKS framework regulate gambling access and consumer protection. Payment regulation captures the institutions that move money. AML regulation requires identification of ultimate beneficial owners, business purpose, source of funds, and suspicious activity.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe cluster\u2019s architecture appears to split these responsibilities:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nthe offshore parent holds gambling rights or claims offshore licensing;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe Cyprus vehicle presents an EU-facing corporate layer;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe employee-director and fiduciary-service provider create a formal local governance structure;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe open-banking provider supplies payment-initiation infrastructure;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe gateway or orchestration layer may route transactions;<\/li>\n\n\n\nthe EMI or banking endpoint may settle funds;<\/li>\n\n\n\neach layer can argue that compliance responsibility lies elsewhere.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nEU law does not permit regulated firms to outsource responsibility into invisibility.<\/p>\n\n\n\nYapily, as a regulated PISP or AIS\/PIS provider, ISX Financial, if involved, as an EMI, and any payment-orchestration provider, if regulated or contractually responsible, each face independent obligations around onboarding, KYB, AML monitoring, suspicious-activity escalation, and use-case control.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhere the ultimate commercial activity is offshore-licensed casino gambling targeting restricted markets, every regulated institution in the chain faces the same question:<\/p>\n\n\n\nWhat did you know, what should your KYB have revealed, and what did you report?<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nRegulatory Questions Triggered by the Restructurings<\/h2>\n\n\n\nThe 10 October 2023 coordinated handover of Gadzooks Limited into the P.Z.T. orbit, and similar fiduciary migrations identified in other pillars, should have triggered enhanced scrutiny by every regulated institution dealing with these companies.<\/p>\n\n\n\nFor banks, EMIs, PSPs, PISPs, auditors, and corporate-service providers, such a change is not routine when the customer is connected to offshore gambling activity.<\/p>\n\n\n\nKey questions include:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nWas the director change reviewed as a material change in governance?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWas the secretary change reviewed as a change in control environment?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWas the offshore owner identified and risk-rated?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWere casino activities disclosed to banks and payment providers?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWere restricted-market risks identified?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWere Dutch, Belgian, Austrian, German, Italian, or other EU consumer flows detected?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWere SARs \/ STRs filed where required?<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nIf the answer is \u201cno,\u201d the issue is not only the casino. It is the entire compliance chain.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nCompliance Risk Assessment<\/h2>\n\n\n\nRisk category<\/th>Rating<\/th>Indicators<\/th>Open questions<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>AML \/ beneficial ownership<\/td>High<\/td>Cura\u00e7ao ownership, Cyprus vehicle, employee-director, recurring fiduciary hub<\/td>Who ultimately controls the structure and receives the proceeds?<\/td><\/tr>Gambling regulation<\/td>High<\/td>Offshore licence environment, Dutch-facing signals, no apparent KSA licence<\/td>Which EU markets were targeted and by whom?<\/td><\/tr>Payment services<\/td>High<\/td>Alleged open-banking payment flows, possible gateway\/orchestration layer, potential EMI endpoint<\/td>Which entity was the merchant of record and which regulated firms processed the flows?<\/td><\/tr>Consumer protection<\/td>High<\/td>Reported Dutch player exposure, CRUKS perimeter gap, refund\/withdrawal complaints<\/td>What remedies exist for self-excluded or restricted-market players?<\/td><\/tr>Corporate governance<\/td>High<\/td>Sole employee-director, offshore shareholder, fiduciary-service handover<\/td>Was there real mind and management in Cyprus?<\/td><\/tr>Professional-services risk<\/td>Elevated<\/td>P.Z.T.\/Potens recurrence, Larnaca address, alleged attorney involvement<\/td>Who owns\/controls the service providers and what due diligence was performed?<\/td><\/tr>Yapily-specific issue<\/td>Elevated<\/td>Initial denial, later acknowledgement after bank metadata, alleged silence<\/td>What did Yapily\u2019s internal review conclude?<\/td><\/tr>PAYTECH \/ ISX leads<\/td>Unverified but significant<\/td>Whistleblower allegations<\/td>Require contracts, payment logs, settlement files, and right of reply<\/td><\/tr><\/tbody><\/table><\/figure>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nKey Questions for Regulators and Financial Institutions<\/h2>\n\n\n\nCyprus Registrar of Companies \/ CySEC \/ MOKAS<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\nWho are the beneficial owners of P.Z.T. Services Ltd and Potens Corporate Services Ltd according to UBO filings?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhat declared economic activity does Gadzooks Limited report?<\/li>\n\n\n\nIs the declared activity consistent with a Cyprus company acting as payment agent or payment vehicle for an offshore casino?<\/li>\n\n\n\nHave UBO filings for Gadzooks Limited, IMMIX Solutions Ltd, and S.I.L.V.E.R Veil Group Ltd been verified against economic substance?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWere the coordinated director\/secretary changes reviewed by any AML supervisor?<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\nDutch Kansspelautoriteit (KSA)<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\nWhat is the enforcement status of Trueluck, Luckywayz, Gadzooks-linked casino brands, and associated domains with respect to the Dutch market?<\/li>\n\n\n\nHas the KSA reviewed Dutch-language acquisition funnels, affiliate pages, and payment methods?<\/li>\n\n\n\nHave payment-blocking or affiliate-enforcement measures been initiated?<\/li>\n\n\n\nHave Dutch self-excluded players reported losses through these brands?<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\nYapily \/ Yapily Connect UAB<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\nThrough which merchant, platform, or partner relationship did the alleged Gadzooks \/ Trueluck payment flows enter Yapily\u2019s rails?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhat KYB and use-case review was performed?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWas the relevant payment flow classified as gambling-related?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhat merchant category coding, descriptor, or payment-purpose data was available?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhy did the initial response reportedly state that no transactions could be found?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhat was the outcome of the escalated internal investigation?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWere suspicious-activity reports filed?<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\nISX Financial EU PLC<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\nDid ISX Financial provide accounts, settlement services, EMI services, or payment infrastructure to Gadzooks, Luckywayz, Trueluck, PAYTECH, or related entities?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhat KYB was performed?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWas Luckywayz Limited B.V. identified as the majority shareholder of Gadzooks?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhat merchant category coding and descriptors were used?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWere restricted-market gambling risks identified?<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\nPAYTECH LTD<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\nDoes PAYTECH maintain any contractual or technical relationship with Gadzooks Limited, Trueluck, Luckywayz, or related casino brands?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhat regulatory status does PAYTECH hold?<\/li>\n\n\n\nDid PAYTECH provide smart routing, checkout, cascading, provider-switching, or payment-orchestration services?<\/li>\n\n\n\nWhich PSPs, EMIs, PISPs, banks, or acquirers were connected?<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\nCyprus Bar Association<\/h3>\n\n\n\n\nWhat professional-conduct rules apply to attorneys who own, control, advise, or operate corporate-service providers administering high-risk offshore gambling vehicles?<\/li>\n\n\n\nHow are conflicts, nominee-director arrangements, and AML obligations supervised?<\/li>\n\n\n\nIs there an obligation to re-evaluate client risk when a company moves into casino\/payment activity after incorporation?<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nCall for Whistleblowers<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram seeks information from current and former insiders across all three pillars of this cluster, including:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nemployees, contractors, and former staff of P.Z.T. active compliance investigation into Softon Ltd<\/strong>, a private Cyprus company incorporated in August 2024, which is publicly identified as the owner and operator of online casino brands operating under an Anjouan\/Comoros offshore licence.<\/p>\n\n\n\nOur investigation focuses on the role that Cyprus-registered corporate vehicles, legal-service providers, and EU-based payment infrastructure play in enabling offshore-licensed casino operations to reach EU consumers \u2014 including consumers in jurisdictions where such operations are prohibited without a local licence.<\/p>\n\n\n\nThe investigation covers the following areas:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nThe corporate structure and beneficial ownership of Softon Ltd (HE 463977)<\/strong>, Nicosia<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe relationship between Softon Ltd and online casino brands including Betzter.com<\/strong> and KingdomCasino<\/strong>-branded domains<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe role of EU open-banking infrastructure<\/strong> and intermediary payment gateways in processing deposits from EU consumers into unlicensed offshore casino structures<\/li>\n\n\n\nCross-border network indicators connecting Cyprus and Estonia<\/strong> in the payment architecture<\/li>\n\n\n\nThe systemic question of whether Cyprus corporate and legal-service ecosystems are functioning as a regulatory arbitrage layer<\/strong> that structurally undermines enforcement by other EU member states, including the Netherlands<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nThank You to Our Whistleblowers<\/h2>\n\n\n\nWe have already received substantive submissions from multiple whistleblowers, including documentary evidence from official Cyprus and Estonian company registries, website captures, payment-flow material, and first-hand accounts.<\/p>\n\n\n\nTo those who have already submitted: your contributions are being taken seriously and are actively informing our investigation.<\/strong> We cannot respond individually to anonymous submissions \u2014 this is by design, to protect your identity \u2014 but every submission is reviewed by our editorial team and treated with full source protection.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIf you have submitted material and are wondering whether it reached us or whether it was useful: it did, and it was.<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nWe Need More<\/h2>\n\n\n\nInvestigations of this kind depend on insider evidence that public registries and website captures alone cannot provide. We are specifically looking for:<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nInternal documents and contracts<\/strong> relating to Softon Ltd, its casino brands, or associated payment entities<\/li>\n\n\n\nPayment-flow evidence<\/strong> \u2014 settlement reports, account statements, transaction records, or payment-descriptor data linking Softon Ltd or its casino brands to specific banks, gateways, or open-banking providers<\/li>\n\n\n\nKYC\/KYB files and onboarding correspondence<\/strong> between Softon Ltd or associated casino brands and payment service providers, banks, or open-banking platforms<\/li>\n\n\n\nCustomer-complaint records and withdrawal-handling procedures<\/strong> \u2014 particularly any internal instructions regarding refund denials or jurisdiction-based exclusion clauses<\/li>\n\n\n\nInternal legal or compliance memoranda<\/strong>, including any AML red-flag assessments or risk opinions produced in connection with Softon Ltd or its network<\/li>\n\n\n\nLicensing representations<\/strong> made to banks, payment providers, or regulators<\/li>\n\n\n\nBeneficial ownership information<\/strong> \u2014 any documentation identifying the true economic beneficiaries behind Softon Ltd or associated entities<\/li>\n\n\n\nEmployee or contractor accounts<\/strong> \u2014 if you have worked for, with, or alongside any entity in this network in any capacity, your perspective is relevant<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nHow to Submit<\/h2>\n\n\n\nAll submissions are handled through Whistle42<\/strong>, FinTelegram’s secure and fully confidential whistleblower channel.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\nAnonymous submissions are accepted and encouraged<\/li>\n\n\n\nNo identifying metadata is collected or stored<\/li>\n\n\n\nSource protection is a non-negotiable editorial principle at FinTelegram<\/li>\n\n\n\nSubmissions can include documents, screenshots, email correspondence, or written accounts<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n\n\n\nSubmit via Whistle42: [whistle42.com]<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\nIf you have already submitted and wish to add further material, please use the same secure channel. There is no need to identify yourself or reference your earlier submission \u2014 our team will connect the information.<\/p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nWhat Happens Next<\/h2>\n\n\n\nFinTelegram is preparing a full compliance intelligence report on Softon Ltd<\/strong> and its network. The report will be published following completion of our editorial verification process and the right-of-reply procedure extended to relevant parties.<\/p>\n\n\n\nWe are committed to accurate, evidence-based reporting. Every factual claim in the final report will be attributed to a verifiable source. Persons and entities named will be given the opportunity to respond before publication, and any substantiated corrections or statements will be published in full.<\/p>\n\n\n\nIf you have evidence that supports, contradicts, or adds nuance to any aspect of this investigation \u2014 we want to hear from you, regardless of which side of the story you are on.<\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n\n\nShare Information via Whistle42<\/a><\/div>\n<\/div>\n","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"FinTelegram has opened a formal compliance investigation into Softon Ltd (HE 463977), a Cyprus-registered company linked to offshore-licensed online casino brands actively targeting EU consumers.
